# Tools reference

_Generated by `npm run docs` from the registry — 136 tools, s3eidat-tools-mcp 0.1.0. Do not edit by hand._

Examples were run with the clock set to 2026-01-15 09:30 UTC. File inputs take a local path or base64; file outputs go to `output` (a file, or a folder ending in `/`) or a temp folder, and the result returns the path. Everything runs locally.

<a id="pdf_compress"></a>
### `pdf_compress` — Compress PDF

Shrink a PDF by re-rendering every page as a JPEG image (best for scans and photo-heavy files). Presets like the website: email (96 DPI, quality 55), balanced (144 DPI, 72), high (220 DPI, 85); or set dpi/quality yourself; optional grayscale. The result loses selectable text, links and form fields. Reports before/after size and a verdict ("bigger" means keep the original: the PDF was already efficient). Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/pdf-compress/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `file` | string | yes | The PDF: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI). |
| `preset` | "email" \| "balanced" \| "high" | no (default `"balanced"`) | Quality preset. Default "balanced". |
| `dpi` | integer | no | Override the preset resolution (36-300 DPI). |
| `quality` | integer | no | Override the preset JPEG quality (30-95). |
| `grayscale` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Convert pages to grayscale. |
| `password` | string | no | Password, if the PDF is protected. |
| `output` | string | no | Where to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set). |
| `returnBase64` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `path` | string | Absolute path of the written PDF. |
| `bytes` | number | Size of the written PDF in bytes. |
| `mimeType` | string |  |
| `base64` | string |  |
| `pages` | number |  |
| `before` | number | Original size in bytes. |
| `after` | number | Compressed size in bytes. |
| `percentSmaller` | number | How much smaller, in % (negative when bigger). |
| `verdict` | "smaller" \| "same" \| "bigger" | "same" = under 5% saved; "bigger" = keep the original. |
| `dpi` | number |  |
| `quality` | number |  |

**Example** — small enough to attach to an email

```json
{
  "file": "scan.pdf",
  "preset": "email"
}
```

<a id="pdf_extract_pages"></a>
### `pdf_extract_pages` — Extract PDF pages

Make one new PDF from selected pages of a PDF, e.g. "1-3, 7, 10-" (order as written, each page once). The rest of the file is left out. To reorder, duplicate or delete pages use pdf_organize. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/pdf-split/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `file` | string | yes | The PDF: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI). |
| `pages` | string | yes | Pages to keep: "1-3, 7, 10-" ("5-" = to the end; also "odd", "even", "last"). |
| `output` | string | no | Where to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set). |
| `returnBase64` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `path` | string | Absolute path of the written PDF. |
| `bytes` | number | Size of the written PDF in bytes. |
| `mimeType` | string |  |
| `base64` | string |  |
| `pages` | number[] | 1-based source pages, in output order. |

**Example** — writes report-pages-1-3_7.pdf

```json
{
  "file": "report.pdf",
  "pages": "1-3, 7"
}
```

<a id="pdf_form_fields"></a>
### `pdf_form_fields` — List PDF form fields

List the fillable fields of a PDF form in reading order: name, readable label, type (text, checkbox, radio, dropdown, list, signature, button), page, current value, options, and whether it is required / read-only / multi-line. Use the names with pdf_form_fill. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/pdf-fill/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `file` | string | yes | The PDF: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI). |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `count` | number |  |
| `xfa` | boolean | The form also has an XFA (LiveCycle) layer; filling removes it. |
| `fields` | object[] |  |

**Example**

```json
{
  "file": "application.pdf"
}
```

<a id="pdf_form_fill"></a>
### `pdf_form_fill` — Fill a PDF form

Fill PDF form fields by name (get names from pdf_form_fields): text as strings, checkboxes as true/false, radio groups and dropdowns by option text, lists as arrays. Optionally flatten (bake the answers into the page, no longer editable). Only changed fields are redrawn. Text the standard font cannot draw (Arabic…) is kept and viewers are asked to draw it, but cannot be flattened. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/pdf-fill/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `file` | string | yes | The PDF: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI). |
| `values` | object | yes | Field name -> value, e.g. {"name": "Sara", "agree": true, "plan": "pro", "langs": ["Arabic"]}. |
| `flatten` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Flatten the form after filling (fields become plain page content). |
| `ignoreUnknown` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Skip names that are not in the form instead of failing. |
| `output` | string | no | Where to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set). |
| `returnBase64` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `path` | string | Absolute path of the written PDF. |
| `bytes` | number | Size of the written PDF in bytes. |
| `mimeType` | string |  |
| `base64` | string |  |
| `filled` | string[] | Fields whose value was set. |
| `unknown` | string[] | Names not found in the form (only with ignoreUnknown). |
| `needAppearances` | string[] | Fields whose text the standard font cannot draw; the viewer draws them. |
| `flattened` | boolean |  |

**Example**

```json
{
  "file": "application.pdf",
  "values": {
    "name": "Sara Haddad",
    "agree": true,
    "plan": "pro",
    "country": "Egypt"
  },
  "flatten": true
}
```

<a id="pdf_from_images"></a>
### `pdf_from_images` — Images to PDF

Make a PDF from images (JPEG, PNG, WebP, GIF, TIFF, AVIF, SVG…), one image per page, in the order given. Page size A4, Letter, or "fit" (page = image size at 96 dpi); orientation auto/portrait/landscape; margin in mm; quality "original" (JPEG/PNG kept as-is), high (≤3000 px, JPEG 90), medium (≤2000 px, 75) or small (≤1400 px, 60). Photos are turned upright from their EXIF orientation. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/images-to-pdf/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `images` | (string \| object)[] | yes | Images in page order: path/base64 strings, or {file, rotate}. |
| `pageSize` | "a4" \| "letter" \| "fit" | no (default `"a4"`) | Page size. Default "a4". |
| `orientation` | "auto" \| "portrait" \| "landscape" | no (default `"auto"`) | "auto" turns the page to match each image. Ignored for "fit". |
| `margin` | number | no (default `10`) | Margin around each image in mm. Default 10. |
| `quality` | "original" \| "high" \| "medium" \| "small" | no (default `"high"`) | Image quality / size trade-off. Default "high". |
| `output` | string | no | Where to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set). |
| `returnBase64` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `path` | string | Absolute path of the written PDF. |
| `bytes` | number | Size of the written PDF in bytes. |
| `mimeType` | string |  |
| `base64` | string |  |
| `pages` | number |  |

**Example**

```json
{
  "images": [
    "receipt1.jpg",
    "receipt2.jpg"
  ],
  "output": "receipts.pdf"
}
```

**Example**

```json
{
  "images": [
    "scan.png",
    {
      "file": "photo.jpg",
      "rotate": 90
    }
  ],
  "pageSize": "fit",
  "margin": 0,
  "quality": "original"
}
```

<a id="pdf_info"></a>
### `pdf_info` — PDF info

Quick facts about a PDF for planning the next step: page count, page sizes (points, mm, paper name such as "A4 portrait", grouped by size), rotated pages, encryption, PDF version, form field count, title/author, and which pages have no text layer (scans that would need OCR). Related web tool: https://s3eidat.github.io/pdf-metadata/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `file` | string | yes | The PDF: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI). |
| `password` | string | no | Password, for the text check on a protected PDF. |
| `textCheck` | boolean | no (default `true`) | Check which pages have a text layer (reads up to 500 pages). Default true. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `pages` | number |  |
| `fileBytes` | number |  |
| `pdfVersion` | string |  |
| `encrypted` | boolean |  |
| `sizes` | object[] | Shown page sizes in points (rotation applied), grouped; "pages" lists the 1-based pages with that size. |
| `rotatedPages` | object[] |  |
| `formFields` | number |  |
| `title` | string \| null |  |
| `author` | string \| null |  |
| `producer` | string \| null |  |
| `pagesWithoutText` | number[] \| null | 1-based pages with no text layer; null when not checked. |
| `textChecked` | number | How many pages the text check looked at. |

**Example** — { pages: 12, sizes: [{ paper: "A4 portrait", pages: "1-12" }], pagesWithoutText: [1, …] }

```json
{
  "file": "scan.pdf"
}
```

<a id="pdf_merge"></a>
### `pdf_merge` — Merge PDFs

Combine several PDFs into one, in the order given. Each entry can be a file (path or base64) or {file, pages} to take only some pages ("1-3, 5, 8-"; "3-1" keeps reverse order; a page can repeat). Password-protected PDFs must be unlocked first. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/pdf-merge/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `files` | (string \| object)[] | yes | PDFs to merge, in order: a path/base64 string, or {file, pages}. |
| `output` | string | no | Where to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set). |
| `returnBase64` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `path` | string | Absolute path of the written PDF. |
| `bytes` | number | Size of the written PDF in bytes. |
| `mimeType` | string |  |
| `base64` | string |  |
| `pages` | number | Pages in the merged PDF. |
| `sources` | object[] |  |

**Example** — report + pages 2-4 of the appendix

```json
{
  "files": [
    "report.pdf",
    {
      "file": "appendix.pdf",
      "pages": "2-4"
    }
  ],
  "output": "merged.pdf"
}
```

<a id="pdf_metadata_read"></a>
### `pdf_metadata_read` — Read PDF metadata

Show what a PDF reveals about itself: Title, Author, Subject, Keywords, Creator, Producer, creation/modification dates (with time zone), custom info fields, XMP metadata (size, edit history, creator) and the document ID. Optionally returns the XMP XML. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/pdf-metadata/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `file` | string | yes | The PDF: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI). |
| `includeXmp` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Also return the decoded XMP XML. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `pages` | number |  |
| `pdfVersion` | string |  |
| `fileBytes` | number |  |
| `fields` | object | Title, Author, Subject, Keywords, Creator, Producer ("" when empty). |
| `dates` | object | CreationDate and ModDate. |
| `custom` | object | Non-standard info fields (Company, SourceModified…). |
| `xmp` | object \| null |  |
| `pageLevelMetadata` | number | Pages (plus the catalog) carrying their own XMP/PieceInfo. |
| `documentId` | boolean | Whether the trailer has a document ID (a fingerprint linking copies). |
| `xml` | string \| null |  |

**Example**

```json
{
  "file": "contract.pdf"
}
```

<a id="pdf_metadata_write"></a>
### `pdf_metadata_write` — Edit or strip PDF metadata

Change PDF info fields (title, author, subject, keywords, creator, producer, dates, custom fields) or strip all metadata: info fields, XMP, page-level data and the document ID, and drop unreachable leftovers from earlier edits so no trace stays in the bytes. Untouched values keep their exact original form. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/pdf-metadata/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `file` | string | yes | The PDF: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI). |
| `strip` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Remove ALL metadata (other edits are ignored). |
| `title` | string | no | Title. "" removes it; omit to keep it. |
| `author` | string | no | Author. "" removes it; omit to keep it. |
| `subject` | string | no | Subject. "" removes it; omit to keep it. |
| `keywords` | string | no | Keywords. "" removes it; omit to keep it. |
| `creator` | string | no | Creator (the app that made it). "" removes it; omit to keep it. |
| `producer` | string | no | Producer (the PDF engine). "" removes it; omit to keep it. |
| `creationDate` | string | no | Creation date as ISO 8601, e.g. "2026-03-14T09:30:00+03:00". "" removes it; omit to keep it. |
| `modDate` | string | no | Modification date as ISO 8601. "" removes it; omit to keep it. |
| `custom` | object | no | Custom info fields to set; null removes one. E.g. {"Company": null}. |
| `removeXmp` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Remove the XMP metadata stream (and page-level XMP/PieceInfo). |
| `removeDocumentId` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Remove the document ID from the trailer. |
| `output` | string | no | Where to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set). |
| `returnBase64` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `path` | string | Absolute path of the written PDF. |
| `bytes` | number | Size of the written PDF in bytes. |
| `mimeType` | string |  |
| `base64` | string |  |
| `removedObjects` | number | Unreachable objects dropped. |
| `metadata` | object |  |

**Example**

```json
{
  "file": "report.pdf",
  "title": "Final report",
  "author": "Sara Haddad"
}
```

**Example** — writes cv-clean.pdf with no metadata

```json
{
  "file": "cv.pdf",
  "strip": true
}
```

<a id="pdf_organize"></a>
### `pdf_organize` — Organize PDF pages

Rebuild a PDF from a page list: reorder, delete (leave pages out), duplicate (list a page twice) and insert blank pages. "3, 1-2, blank, 5-" puts page 3 first, then 1-2, a blank page (same size as the page before it), then 5 to the end; page 4 is dropped. Optionally rotate pages of the result. Existing page rotation is kept. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/pdf-organizer/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `file` | string | yes | The PDF: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI). |
| `pages` | string | yes | New page order (1-based source pages): e.g. "3, 1-2, blank, 2, 5-". Pages not listed are removed; "blank" adds an empty page. |
| `rotate` | object[] | no | Optional rotations applied to pages of the new order. |
| `output` | string | no | Where to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set). |
| `returnBase64` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `path` | string | Absolute path of the written PDF. |
| `bytes` | number | Size of the written PDF in bytes. |
| `mimeType` | string |  |
| `base64` | string |  |
| `pages` | number | Pages in the result. |
| `order` | (number \| "blank")[] | Source page (1-based) of each output page, or "blank". |

**Example** — swap the first two pages, turn page 3

```json
{
  "file": "scan.pdf",
  "pages": "2, 1, 3-",
  "rotate": [
    {
      "pages": "3",
      "angle": 90
    }
  ]
}
```

<a id="pdf_page_numbers"></a>
### `pdf_page_numbers` — Add page numbers

Add page numbers to a PDF, plus an optional header and footer line. Format tokens: {n}, {total}, {date}, {filename} (e.g. "Page {n} of {total}", "{n} / {total}", "- {n} -"). Choose the corner/edge, start number, how many leading pages to skip (e.g. a cover), a page range, font, size, colour and margin. Numbers stay upright on rotated pages. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/pdf-page-numbers/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `file` | string | yes | The PDF: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI). |
| `format` | string | no (default `"Page {n} of {total}"`) | Number format with {n}, {total}, {date}, {filename}. Default "Page {n} of {total}". Empty = no number (header/footer only). |
| `position` | "tl" \| "tc" \| "tr" \| "bl" \| "bc" \| "br" | no (default `"bc"`) | Where the number goes: t/b = top/bottom, l/c/r = left/centre/right. Default "bc". |
| `start` | integer | no (default `1`) | Number of the first numbered page. Default 1. |
| `skip` | integer | no (default `0`) | Leave this many first pages without a number (e.g. 1 for a cover). Default 0. |
| `pages` | string | no | Pages to number: 1-based list like "1-3, 5, 8-" ("5-" = to the end, "-3" = first three, "3-1" = reversed). Also "all", "odd", "even", "last". Empty = all pages. |
| `font` | "Helvetica" \| "HelveticaBold" \| "TimesRoman" \| "Courier" | no (default `"Helvetica"`) |  |
| `size` | number | no (default `10`) | Text size in points. Default 10. |
| `color` | string | no (default `"#333333"`) | Colour as hex "#rrggbb". Default #333333. |
| `margin` | number | no (default `28`) | Distance from the page edge in points. Default 28. |
| `header` | string | no (default `""`) | Optional header text on numbered pages (tokens allowed). |
| `headerAlign` | "l" \| "c" \| "r" | no (default `"c"`) | Header alignment: l, c or r. |
| `footer` | string | no (default `""`) | Optional footer text on numbered pages (tokens allowed). |
| `footerAlign` | "l" \| "c" \| "r" | no (default `"c"`) | Footer alignment: l, c or r. |
| `date` | string | no | Text for {date}. Default: today in the server locale. |
| `output` | string | no | Where to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set). |
| `returnBase64` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `path` | string | Absolute path of the written PDF. |
| `bytes` | number | Size of the written PDF in bytes. |
| `mimeType` | string |  |
| `base64` | string |  |
| `numbered` | number | How many pages got a number. |
| `first` | number \| null |  |
| `last` | number \| null |  |

**Example** — no number on the cover

```json
{
  "file": "report.pdf",
  "format": "Page {n} of {total}",
  "position": "br",
  "skip": 1
}
```

**Example**

```json
{
  "file": "thesis.pdf",
  "format": "{n}",
  "footer": "Draft, {date}",
  "footerAlign": "l"
}
```

<a id="pdf_rotate"></a>
### `pdf_rotate` — Rotate PDF pages

Rotate selected pages of a PDF by 90, 180 or 270 degrees clockwise (-90 = counter-clockwise), on top of any rotation they already have. Other pages, links and form fields are left as they are. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/pdf-organizer/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `file` | string | yes | The PDF: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI). |
| `angle` | 90 \| 180 \| 270 \| -90 | yes | Clockwise degrees: 90, 180, 270, or -90. |
| `pages` | string | no | Pages to rotate: 1-based list like "1-3, 5, 8-" ("5-" = to the end, "-3" = first three, "3-1" = reversed). Also "all", "odd", "even", "last". Empty = all pages. |
| `output` | string | no | Where to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set). |
| `returnBase64` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `path` | string | Absolute path of the written PDF. |
| `bytes` | number | Size of the written PDF in bytes. |
| `mimeType` | string |  |
| `base64` | string |  |
| `rotated` | number[] | 1-based pages that were rotated. |
| `rotations` | number[] | Final /Rotate of every page, in degrees. |

**Example**

```json
{
  "file": "scan.pdf",
  "angle": 90,
  "pages": "even"
}
```

<a id="pdf_sign_image"></a>
### `pdf_sign_image` — Sign PDF with an image

Place a signature (or initials, stamp, logo) image on PDF pages at x/y/width in points, measured from the top-left of the page as it is shown (rotated pages are handled). A light paper background is removed by default so scanned signatures sit cleanly on the page. Optional text marks (name, date…) can be added too. This is a visual signature, not a cryptographic digital signature. To draw a signature by hand, use the web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/pdf-sign/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `file` | string | yes | The PDF: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI). |
| `signature` | string | no | Signature image: local path or base64 (PNG, JPEG, WebP…). |
| `pages` | string | no (default `"last"`) | Pages to sign: "last" (default), "1", "all", "1-3"… |
| `x` | number | no (default `72`) | Left edge in points from the left of the page (72 pt = 1 inch). Default 72. |
| `y` | number | no | Top edge in points from the TOP of the page. Default: 72 pt above the bottom margin. |
| `width` | number | no (default `150`) | Width in points. Default 150 (about 5.3 cm). |
| `height` | number | no | Height in points. Default: keeps the image aspect ratio. |
| `removeBackground` | boolean | no (default `true`) | Make light (paper) pixels transparent and trim to the ink. Default true. |
| `threshold` | integer | no (default `210`) | Brightness (0-255) above which pixels become transparent. Default 210. |
| `texts` | object[] | no | Optional text marks, e.g. the printed name or the date. |
| `output` | string | no | Where to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set). |
| `returnBase64` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `path` | string | Absolute path of the written PDF. |
| `bytes` | number | Size of the written PDF in bytes. |
| `mimeType` | string |  |
| `base64` | string |  |
| `signedPages` | number[] |  |
| `signature` | object \| null | Placed size in points. |

**Example**

```json
{
  "file": "contract.pdf",
  "signature": "signature.png",
  "pages": "last",
  "x": 360,
  "y": 700,
  "width": 160
}
```

**Example**

```json
{
  "file": "form.pdf",
  "signature": "sig.jpg",
  "pages": "2",
  "x": 80,
  "y": 620,
  "texts": [
    {
      "text": "29/10/2026",
      "page": 2,
      "x": 300,
      "y": 640
    }
  ]
}
```

<a id="pdf_split"></a>
### `pdf_split` — Split PDF

Split one PDF into several PDFs written to a folder: by page ranges (one file per range, e.g. "1-3, 4-6, 7-"), every N pages, or one file per page. Files are named like the website: <name>-pages-1-3.pdf, <name>-part-01.pdf, <name>-page-001.pdf. Use pdf_extract_pages to get a single PDF with chosen pages. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/pdf-split/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `file` | string | yes | The PDF: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI). |
| `mode` | "ranges" \| "every" \| "single" | yes | "ranges": one PDF per range in `ranges`; "every": a new PDF every `every` pages; "single": one PDF per page. |
| `ranges` | string | no | For mode "ranges": comma-separated ranges, each becomes one PDF, e.g. "1-3, 4-6, 7-". |
| `every` | integer | no | For mode "every": pages per file (e.g. 2 makes 1-2, 3-4…). |
| `output` | string | no | Folder to write the PDFs into (created if missing). Default: a new folder in the server output folder. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `folder` | string | Folder the PDFs were written to. |
| `files` | object[] |  |

**Example** — three PDFs

```json
{
  "file": "book.pdf",
  "mode": "ranges",
  "ranges": "1-10, 11-20, 21-"
}
```

**Example**

```json
{
  "file": "scans.pdf",
  "mode": "every",
  "every": 2
}
```

<a id="pdf_to_images"></a>
### `pdf_to_images` — PDF to images

Render PDF pages to PNG, JPEG or WebP images in a folder (one file per page, named <name>-p001.png…). Choose the DPI (72 = screen size, 150 = default, 300 = print), JPEG/WebP quality, a page range, and a transparent background for PNG/WebP. Very large pages are capped at 16384 px a side / 16.7 megapixels, like the website. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/pdf-to-images/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `file` | string | yes | The PDF: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI). |
| `format` | "png" \| "jpeg" \| "webp" | no (default `"png"`) | Image format. Default "png". |
| `dpi` | number | no (default `150`) | Resolution in dots per inch. Default 150. |
| `quality` | integer | no (default `90`) | JPEG/WebP quality 1-100. Default 90. |
| `transparent` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Keep the page background transparent (PNG/WebP only). |
| `pages` | string | no | Pages to render: 1-based list like "1-3, 5, 8-" ("5-" = to the end, "-3" = first three, "3-1" = reversed). Also "all", "odd", "even", "last". Empty = all pages. |
| `password` | string | no | Password, if the PDF is protected. |
| `output` | string | no | Folder to write the images into (created if missing). Default: a new folder in the server output folder. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `folder` | string |  |
| `files` | object[] |  |

**Example**

```json
{
  "file": "slides.pdf",
  "format": "jpeg",
  "dpi": 96,
  "pages": "1-5"
}
```

**Example**

```json
{
  "file": "logo.pdf",
  "format": "png",
  "dpi": 300,
  "transparent": true
}
```

<a id="pdf_to_text"></a>
### `pdf_to_text` — PDF to text

Extract the text of a PDF with lines and paragraphs rebuilt (right-to-left Arabic/Hebrew lines in reading order), as plain text or Markdown, with optional page separators and reflow (join lines, mend hyphenated words). Returns per-page word counts and flags pages with no text layer (scans: this tool does no OCR). Supports page ranges and passwords. Give "output" to write a .txt/.md file instead of returning the text inline. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/pdf-to-text/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `file` | string | yes | The PDF: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI). |
| `pages` | string | no | Pages to read: 1-based list like "1-3, 5, 8-" ("5-" = to the end, "-3" = first three, "3-1" = reversed). Also "all", "odd", "even", "last". Empty = all pages. |
| `password` | string | no | Password, if the PDF is protected. |
| `format` | "text" \| "markdown" | no (default `"text"`) | "text" or "markdown" (## Page N headings, reflowed paragraphs). |
| `separators` | boolean | no (default `true`) | Put a "——— Page N ———" line before each page. Default true. |
| `reflow` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Join the lines of each paragraph and mend words split by a hyphen. |
| `perPage` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Also return each page's text separately. |
| `maxChars` | integer | no (default `200000`) | Cut the inline text after this many characters (the file output is never cut). |
| `output` | string | no | Write the text to this file (or folder ending in "/") instead of returning it inline. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `pageCount` | number | Pages in the PDF. |
| `words` | number |  |
| `characters` | number |  |
| `pagesWithText` | number |  |
| `pagesWithoutText` | number[] | 1-based pages with no text layer (probably scanned images). |
| `pages` | object[] |  |
| `text` | string |  |
| `truncated` | boolean |  |
| `path` | string |  |
| `bytes` | number |  |

**Example**

```json
{
  "file": "article.pdf",
  "reflow": true
}
```

**Example**

```json
{
  "file": "book.pdf",
  "pages": "1-20",
  "format": "markdown",
  "output": "book.md"
}
```

<a id="pdf_watermark"></a>
### `pdf_watermark` — Watermark PDF

Stamp a text or image watermark on PDF pages: centred or tiled across the page, with opacity, angle, size, colour and a page range. It reads upright on rotated pages. Text the standard PDF fonts cannot draw (Arabic, CJK…) is stamped as an image. Defaults match the website: "CONFIDENTIAL", Helvetica Bold 72 pt, #d64545, 25% opacity, 45°. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/pdf-watermark/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `file` | string | yes | The PDF: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI). |
| `text` | string | no | Watermark text (default "CONFIDENTIAL"). Ignored when "image" is given. |
| `image` | string | no | Image watermark instead of text: a local path or base64 (PNG, JPEG, WebP, SVG…). |
| `font` | "HelveticaBold" \| "Helvetica" \| "TimesRomanBold" \| "CourierBold" | no (default `"HelveticaBold"`) | Font for text. |
| `size` | number | no (default `72`) | Text size in points. Default 72. |
| `color` | string | no (default `"#d64545"`) | Colour as hex "#rrggbb". Default #d64545. |
| `scale` | number | no (default `40`) | Image width as % of the page width. Default 40. |
| `opacity` | number | no (default `25`) | Opacity in % (1-100). Default 25. |
| `angle` | number | no (default `45`) | Rotation in degrees, counter-clockwise as shown. Default 45. |
| `mode` | "center" \| "tile" | no (default `"center"`) | "center": one stamp in the middle; "tile": repeated across the page. |
| `gap` | number | no (default `80`) | Space between tiles in points (tile mode). Default 80. |
| `pages` | string | no | Pages to stamp: 1-based list like "1-3, 5, 8-" ("5-" = to the end, "-3" = first three, "3-1" = reversed). Also "all", "odd", "even", "last". Empty = all pages. |
| `output` | string | no | Where to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set). |
| `returnBase64` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `path` | string | Absolute path of the written PDF. |
| `bytes` | number | Size of the written PDF in bytes. |
| `mimeType` | string |  |
| `base64` | string |  |
| `pagesStamped` | number |  |

**Example**

```json
{
  "file": "roadmap.pdf",
  "text": "DRAFT",
  "opacity": 20
}
```

**Example**

```json
{
  "file": "brochure.pdf",
  "image": "logo.png",
  "mode": "tile",
  "scale": 15,
  "angle": 0,
  "opacity": 10
}
```

<a id="image"></a>
## Images

Convert, resize, crop, compress, watermark, EXIF, palettes, favicons, placeholders.

| Tool | What it does |
|---|---|
| [`image_ascii_art`](#image_ascii_art) | Image to ASCII art |
| [`image_ascii_banner`](#image_ascii_banner) | Text to ASCII banner |
| [`image_compress`](#image_compress) | Compress image |
| [`image_convert`](#image_convert) | Convert image format |
| [`image_crop`](#image_crop) | Crop image |
| [`image_exif_read`](#image_exif_read) | Read photo metadata (EXIF) |
| [`image_exif_strip`](#image_exif_strip) | Remove photo metadata (EXIF) |
| [`image_favicon_set`](#image_favicon_set) | Favicon set generator |
| [`image_from_base64`](#image_from_base64) | Base64 / data URI to image file |
| [`image_palette`](#image_palette) | Colour palette from image |
| [`image_placeholder`](#image_placeholder) | Placeholder image |
| [`image_resize`](#image_resize) | Resize image |
| [`image_rotate`](#image_rotate) | Rotate / flip image |
| [`image_svg_to_png`](#image_svg_to_png) | SVG to PNG |
| [`image_to_base64`](#image_to_base64) | Image to Base64 / data URI |
| [`image_watermark`](#image_watermark) | Watermark image |

<a id="image_ascii_art"></a>
### `image_ascii_art` — Image to ASCII art

Turn an image into ASCII art: each character cell takes the average brightness of its area (cells are twice as tall as wide) and maps it onto a character ramp: standard " .:-=+*#%@", detailed (70 characters), blocks " ░▒▓█", simple, binary, or your own. Contrast, brightness and invert (for light text on dark backgrounds). Returns the text; format "png" also renders it to an image (optionally in the image's colours). Transparent areas count as white paper. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/ascii-art/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `image` | string | yes | The image: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI). |
| `columns` | integer | no (default `100`) | Characters per line. Default 100. |
| `ramp` | "standard" \| "detailed" \| "blocks" \| "simple" \| "binary" \| "custom" | no (default `"standard"`) | Character set, light → dark. Default standard. |
| `customRamp` | string | no (default `" .oO0@"`) | Characters for ramp "custom", from no ink to most ink (at least 2). |
| `contrast` | number | no (default `0`) | -100 … 100. Default 0. |
| `brightness` | number | no (default `0`) | -100 … 100. Default 0. |
| `invert` | boolean | no (default `false`) | For light text on a dark background. Default false. |
| `format` | "text" \| "png" | no (default `"text"`) | text (default) or png (also writes <name>-ascii.png). |
| `color` | boolean | no (default `false`) | PNG only: colour each character with the image colour. |
| `output` | string | no | Where to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set). |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `text` | string |  |
| `columns` | number |  |
| `rows` | number |  |
| `path` | string | Written .txt or .png file, if any. |

**Example**

```json
{
  "image": "/photos/cat.jpg",
  "columns": 80,
  "contrast": 20
}
```

**Example**

```json
{
  "image": "/photos/logo.png",
  "ramp": "blocks",
  "format": "png",
  "color": true
}
```

<a id="image_ascii_banner"></a>
### `image_ascii_banner` — Text to ASCII banner

Big banner letters from text, using the website's own bitmap fonts: block (█ 5×7), char (any fill character, e.g. #), shadow (█ with ░ shade), compact (half-block 5×7), small (█ 3×5), tiny (half-block 3×5). Letters A-Z, digits and common punctuation (unknown characters show as "?"); one banner per input line. Returns the text; format "png" also renders it to banner.png. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/ascii-art/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `text` | string | yes | Text to render (case-insensitive). New lines make separate banners. |
| `style` | "block" \| "char" \| "shadow" \| "compact" \| "small" \| "tiny" | no (default `"block"`) | Letter style. Default block. |
| `fill` | string | no (default `"#"`) | Fill character(s) for style "char". Default "#". |
| `format` | "text" \| "png" | no (default `"text"`) | text (default) or png (also writes banner.png). |
| `theme` | "dark" \| "light" | no (default `"dark"`) | PNG colours: dark (teal on near-black, default) or light. |
| `output` | string | no | Where to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set). |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `text` | string |  |
| `lines` | number |  |
| `path` | string |  |

**Example** — A 7-line banner: "##      ##  ######" …

```json
{
  "text": "HI",
  "style": "char",
  "fill": "#"
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "lines": 7
}
```

<a id="image_compress"></a>
### `image_compress` — Compress image

Make an image file smaller: re-encode at a quality (default 80) and optionally cap the longest side (maxSide), as JPEG, WebP, AVIF or PNG ("keep" = same format; GIF/BMP/TIFF become PNG). Or give targetBytes to find the highest quality that fits that size. With keepSmaller (default on) the original is kept when re-encoding would not make it smaller. Reports before/after sizes and % saved. Transparent areas become white in JPEG. Metadata (EXIF, GPS) is not copied. PNG is lossless (quality ignored). Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/image-squeeze/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `image` | string | yes | The image: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI). |
| `format` | "keep" \| "jpeg" \| "webp" \| "avif" \| "png" | no (default `"keep"`) | Output format. Default keep (same as the input). |
| `quality` | integer | no (default `80`) | Quality 1-100 for JPEG / WebP / AVIF. Default 80 (website default). |
| `maxSide` | integer | no | Scale down so the longest side is at most this many pixels (e.g. 1920). |
| `targetBytes` | integer | no | Aim for a file of at most this many bytes: searches the highest quality that fits (lossy formats only; overrides quality). |
| `keepSmaller` | boolean | no (default `true`) | Keep the original bytes when the result would be larger (same format and size only). Default true. |
| `output` | string | no | Where to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set). |
| `returnBase64` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `path` | string | Absolute path of the written file. |
| `bytes` | number | Size of the written file in bytes. |
| `mimeType` | string |  |
| `base64` | string |  |
| `width` | number | Output width in pixels. |
| `height` | number | Output height in pixels. |
| `format` | string | Output format. |
| `inputBytes` | number | Size of the input file. |
| `savedBytes` | number | Bytes saved (negative when the file grew). |
| `savedPercent` | number | Percent saved, rounded. |
| `quality` | number | Quality used (lossy formats). |
| `kept` | boolean | True when the original was kept because it was already smaller. |
| `targetReached` | boolean | With targetBytes: whether the target was met. |
| `summary` | string |  |

**Example**

```json
{
  "image": "/photos/IMG_0042.jpg",
  "quality": 75,
  "maxSide": 1920
}
```

**Example** — Highest WebP quality that fits 150 KB.

```json
{
  "image": "/site/hero.png",
  "format": "webp",
  "targetBytes": 150000
}
```

<a id="image_convert"></a>
### `image_convert` — Convert image format

Convert an image to PNG, JPEG, WebP, AVIF, GIF or TIFF. Reads JPEG, PNG, WebP, AVIF/HEIF, GIF (first frame), TIFF, SVG, BMP and ICO. Transparent areas are filled with `background` when writing JPEG (or always with `flatten`). EXIF rotation is applied. SVGs are rasterised at their own size (width/height or viewBox; 1024×1024 if neither), scripts never run. Images larger than 16384 px a side or 100 MP are scaled down to fit. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/image-convert/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `image` | string | yes | The image: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI). |
| `format` | "png" \| "jpeg" \| "webp" \| "avif" \| "gif" \| "tiff" | no (default `"png"`) | Output format. Default png (as on the website). |
| `quality` | integer | no (default `92`) | Quality 1-100 for JPEG / WebP / AVIF (ignored for PNG). Default 92, same as the website. |
| `background` | string | no (default `"#ffffff"`) | Colour used for transparent areas when writing JPEG or when flatten is on. Default #ffffff. |
| `flatten` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Fill transparency with the background for every format, not only JPEG. |
| `output` | string | no | Where to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set). |
| `returnBase64` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `path` | string | Absolute path of the written file. |
| `bytes` | number | Size of the written file in bytes. |
| `mimeType` | string |  |
| `base64` | string |  |
| `width` | number | Output width in pixels. |
| `height` | number | Output height in pixels. |
| `format` | string | Output format. |
| `inputFormat` | string | Detected input format. |
| `scaled` | boolean | True when the image was scaled down to fit the size limit. |

**Example** — Writes logo.jpg with transparency on white.

```json
{
  "image": "/photos/logo.png",
  "format": "jpeg",
  "quality": 90,
  "background": "#ffffff"
}
```

**Example**

```json
{
  "image": "/icons/icon.svg",
  "format": "webp"
}
```

<a id="image_crop"></a>
### `image_crop` — Crop image

Crop an image to a pixel box (x, y, width, height) or to an aspect ratio (1:1, 4:5, 3:2, 16:9, 9:16, "W:H" or a number), taking the largest centred region (or aligned to an edge with `position`). Boxes are kept inside the image. Output JPEG/PNG/WebP/AVIF, name <name>-<W>x<H>.<ext>. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/image-resize/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `image` | string | yes | The image: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI). |
| `x` | integer | no | Left edge of the crop box (pixels). Use with y, width, height. |
| `y` | integer | no | Top edge of the crop box (pixels). |
| `width` | integer | no | Width of the crop box (pixels). |
| `height` | integer | no | Height of the crop box (pixels). |
| `ratio` | string \| number | no | Aspect ratio as "W:H" (e.g. "1:1", "4:5", "3:2", "16:9", "9:16"), a number (width/height), "orig" or "free". |
| `position` | "center" \| "top" \| "bottom" \| "left" \| "right" \| "top-left" \| "top-right" \| "bottom-left" \| "bottom-right" | no (default `"center"`) | Where the ratio crop sits. Default center (the website default). |
| `format` | "keep" \| "jpeg" \| "png" \| "webp" \| "avif" | no (default `"keep"`) | Output format. "keep" = same as the input when it is JPEG/PNG/WebP (else PNG). Default keep. |
| `quality` | integer | no (default `90`) | Quality 1-100 for JPEG / WebP / AVIF (ignored for PNG). Default 90, same as the website. |
| `output` | string | no | Where to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set). |
| `returnBase64` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `path` | string | Absolute path of the written file. |
| `bytes` | number | Size of the written file in bytes. |
| `mimeType` | string |  |
| `base64` | string |  |
| `width` | number | Output width in pixels. |
| `height` | number | Output height in pixels. |
| `format` | string | Output format. |
| `crop` | object |  |

**Example** — Largest centred square.

```json
{
  "image": "/photos/group.jpg",
  "ratio": "1:1"
}
```

**Example**

```json
{
  "image": "/photos/group.jpg",
  "x": 100,
  "y": 50,
  "width": 800,
  "height": 600
}
```

<a id="image_exif_read"></a>
### `image_exif_read` — Read photo metadata (EXIF)

Show the hidden metadata in a photo: camera make/model, lens, date taken, exposure, ISO, software, author/copyright, serial numbers, comments, PNG text chunks, and GPS coordinates (where the photo was taken). Lists every metadata block (EXIF, XMP, ICC, IPTC, embedded previews) with its size and flags privacy-sensitive fields. JPEG and PNG fully; WebP/AVIF/HEIC/TIFF EXIF via sharp. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/exif-cleaner/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `image` | string | yes | The photo: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI). |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `format` | string |  |
| `width` | number |  |
| `height` | number |  |
| `fields` | object[] | Readable metadata fields in the website order. |
| `gps` | object \| null | Location the photo reveals, if any. |
| `orientation` | number | EXIF orientation (1 = normal, 6 = rotate 90° clockwise, …). |
| `blocks` | object[] | Metadata blocks found in the file. |
| `hasMetadata` | boolean |  |
| `revealsLocation` | boolean |  |

**Example** — Camera Google Pixel 9, Date taken 2026:10:21 18:42:05, GPS 31.953900, 35.910600.

```json
{
  "image": "/photos/IMG_20261021_184205.jpg"
}
```

<a id="image_exif_strip"></a>
### `image_exif_strip` — Remove photo metadata (EXIF)

Remove EXIF, GPS location, XMP, IPTC, comments, PNG text chunks and embedded previews from a photo before sharing it. JPEG and PNG are cleaned losslessly (image data copied byte for byte, like the website); by default the rotation is kept as a tiny orientation-only EXIF and the ICC colour profile is kept. applyOrientation re-encodes JPEGs with the rotation baked into the pixels. WebP/AVIF/HEIC/TIFF are re-encoded without metadata. Output name <name>-clean.<ext>. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/exif-cleaner/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `image` | string | yes | The photo: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI). |
| `keepOrientation` | boolean | no (default `true`) | Keep only the Orientation tag so the photo still shows the right way up. Default true. |
| `keepIcc` | boolean | no (default `true`) | Keep the ICC colour profile (no personal data; keeps colours right). Default true. |
| `applyOrientation` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Instead of keeping the Orientation tag, rotate the pixels (re-encodes JPEG at `quality`; PNG is unaffected). Default false. |
| `quality` | integer | no (default `92`) | JPEG/WebP/AVIF quality when re-encoding is needed. Default 92. |
| `output` | string | no | Where to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set). |
| `returnBase64` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `path` | string |  |
| `bytes` | number |  |
| `mimeType` | string |  |
| `base64` | string |  |
| `inputBytes` | number |  |
| `format` | string |  |
| `removed` | object[] | Metadata blocks that were removed. |
| `hadLocation` | boolean | True when the original revealed a GPS location. |
| `orientation` | number | Orientation of the input (1 = normal). |
| `reencoded` | boolean | True when the pixels were re-encoded (non-JPEG/PNG input or applyOrientation). |

**Example** — Writes IMG_1234-clean.jpg: no GPS, camera or date; same pixels.

```json
{
  "image": "/photos/IMG_1234.jpg"
}
```

**Example**

```json
{
  "image": "/photos/IMG_1234.jpg",
  "applyOrientation": true
}
```

<a id="image_favicon_set"></a>
### `image_favicon_set` — Favicon set generator

Make a complete favicon set from a letter/emoji/short text or from an image: favicon.ico (16, 32, 48 px), favicon-16x16.png, favicon-32x32.png, apple-touch-icon.png (180, square, solid), android-chrome-192x192.png and -512x512.png, site.webmanifest and head-snippet.html with the <link> tags. Rounded square with background colour (or transparent), corner radius and padding in %. Writes everything into one folder. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/favicon-forge/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `text` | string | no (default `"S"`) | Letter(s) or emoji drawn on the icon (when no image is given). Default "S". |
| `image` | string | no | Use this image (logo) instead of text: a local path or base64. Square, 512 px+ works best. |
| `weight` | "400" \| "500" \| "600" \| "700" \| "800" \| "900" | no (default `"900"`) | Font weight for text. Default 900. |
| `color` | string | no (default `"#ffffff"`) | Text colour. Default #ffffff. |
| `background` | string | no (default `"#0e7c86"`) | Background colour. Default #0e7c86. |
| `transparent` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Transparent background (the Apple icon still gets white). Default false. |
| `radius` | number | no (default `22`) | Corner radius in % of the size (50 = circle). Default 22. |
| `padding` | number | no (default `14`) | Padding around the text/image in %. Default 14. |
| `fit` | "contain" \| "cover" | no (default `"contain"`) | How an image fills the padded square. Default contain. |
| `name` | string | no (default `"My Website"`) | App name for site.webmanifest. Default "My Website". |
| `shortName` | string | no (default `"Site"`) | Short name for site.webmanifest. Default "Site". |
| `output` | string | no | Folder to write the files into. Default: a new "favicons" folder in the output folder. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `folder` | string |  |
| `files` | object[] |  |
| `snippet` | string | The <head> tags to paste. |

**Example**

```json
{
  "text": "S3",
  "background": "#0e7c86",
  "radius": 24,
  "padding": 16,
  "name": "S3eidat Tools",
  "shortName": "S3eidat"
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "folder": "/tmp/s3eidat-tools/favicons/",
  "files": [
    {
      "name": "favicon.ico",
      "path": "/tmp/s3eidat-tools/favicons/favicon.ico",
      "bytes": 4300
    },
    {
      "name": "favicon-16x16.png",
      "path": "/tmp/s3eidat-tools/favicons/favicon-16x16.png",
      "bytes": 635
    },
    {
      "name": "favicon-32x32.png",
      "path": "/tmp/s3eidat-tools/favicons/favicon-32x32.png",
      "bytes": 1366
    },
    {
      "name": "apple-touch-icon.png",
      "path": "/tmp/s3eidat-tools/favicons/apple-touch-icon.png",
      "bytes": 6328
    },
    {
      "name": "android-chrome-192x192.png",
      "path": "/tmp/s3eidat-tools/favicons/android-chrome-192x192.png",
      "bytes": 9404
    },
    {
      "name": "android-chrome-512x512.png",
      "path": "/tmp/s3eidat-tools/favicons/android-chrome-512x512.png",
      "bytes": 19291
    },
    {
      "name": "site.webmanifest",
      "path": "/tmp/s3eidat-tools/favicons/site.webmanifest",
      "bytes": 380
    },
    {
      "name": "head-snippet.html",
      "path": "/tmp/s3eidat-tools/favicons/head-snippet.html",
      "bytes": 368
    }
  ],
  "snippet": "<link rel=\"icon\" href=\"/favicon.ico\" sizes=\"48x48\">\n<link rel=\"icon\" type=\"image/png\" sizes=\"32x32\" href=\"/favicon-32x32.png\">\n<link rel=\"icon\" type=\"image/png\" sizes=\"16x16\" href=\"/favicon-16x16.png\">\n<link rel=\"apple-touch-icon\" sizes=\"180x180\" href=\"/apple-touch-icon.png\">\n<link rel=\"manifest\" href=\"/site.webmanifest\">\n<meta name=\"theme-color\" content=\"#0e7c86\">"
}
```

**Example**

```json
{
  "image": "/brand/logo.png",
  "transparent": true,
  "radius": 0,
  "output": "/site/public/"
}
```

<a id="image_from_base64"></a>
### `image_from_base64` — Base64 / data URI to image file

Decode Base64 back into a file: accepts a data URI, a CSS url("data:…") rule, an <img src="data:…"> tag, URL-encoded SVG data URIs, or raw Base64 (standard or URL-safe, line breaks allowed). Detects the real type from the bytes, warns when it differs from the declared one, and writes decoded.<ext>. Give the text inline or as a path to a text file. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/image-to-base64/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `text` | string | no | The data URI / CSS / <img> tag / raw Base64. |
| `path` | string | no | Or: a text file containing it. |
| `output` | string | no | Where to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set). |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `path` | string |  |
| `bytes` | number |  |
| `mimeType` | string |  |
| `declaredMimeType` | string | Type named in the data URI, if any. |
| `detectedMimeType` | string | Type detected from the bytes. |
| `isImage` | boolean |  |
| `width` | number |  |
| `height` | number |  |
| `status` | string | Same message as the website: "Valid image", or a warning. |

**Example** — Writes decoded.png (1×1).

```json
{
  "text": "data:image/png;base64,iVBORw0KGgoAAAANSUhEUgAAAAEAAAABCAYAAAAfFcSJAAAADUlEQVR42mNk+M9QDwADhgGAWjR9awAAAABJRU5ErkJggg=="
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "path": "/tmp/s3eidat-tools/decoded.png",
  "bytes": 70,
  "mimeType": "image/png",
  "declaredMimeType": "image/png",
  "detectedMimeType": "image/png",
  "isImage": true,
  "width": 1,
  "height": 1,
  "status": "Valid image"
}
```

<a id="image_palette"></a>
### `image_palette` — Colour palette from image

Extract the main colours of an image with their share of the picture: k-means (default, deterministic) or median cut, 1-16 colours, sorted by share. Transparent pixels are ignored. Returns hex + rgb + % per colour, CSS custom properties (:root { --color-1 … }) or JSON, and optionally writes the website's swatch strip as palette.png. Same algorithm and sampling as the website, so the colours match. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/palette-from-image/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `image` | string | yes | The image: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI). |
| `count` | integer | no (default `6`) | Number of colours. Default 6. |
| `method` | "kmeans" \| "median" | no (default `"kmeans"`) | kmeans (default) or median (median cut). |
| `format` | "css" \| "json" | no (default `"css"`) | Export text format. Default css. |
| `swatchPng` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Also write palette.png (160×200 swatch per colour with its hex code). |
| `output` | string | no | Where to write palette.png (file path or folder ending in "/"). Implies swatchPng. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `colors` | object[] |  |
| `text` | string | CSS custom properties or JSON, as exported by the website. |
| `pixelsSampled` | number |  |
| `path` | string | palette.png, when written. |

**Example** — colors: [{ hex: "#1f2f6e", percent: "31.2%" }, …]

```json
{
  "image": "/photos/sunset.jpg",
  "count": 5
}
```

<a id="image_placeholder"></a>
### `image_placeholder` — Placeholder image

Make a placeholder image of any size (up to 8000 px a side): solid background with centred text (default "W × H", "\n" for line breaks), auto-fitted font size or a fixed one, PNG / JPEG / WebP / SVG. Size presets for web, social media, IAB ads and devices. Optionally returns a data URI / <img> tag for inlining. Text uses Inter when installed, else a system sans-serif. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/placeholder-image/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `width` | integer | no (default `1200`) | Width in pixels. Default 1200. |
| `height` | integer | no (default `630`) | Height in pixels. Default 630. |
| `preset` | "open-graph-link-preview" \| "hero-16-9-full-hd" \| "hd-16-9" \| "blog-image-4-3" \| "thumbnail-3-2" \| "avatar" \| "instagram-square" \| "instagram-portrait-4-5" \| "story-reel-9-16" \| "x-twitter-header" \| "linkedin-banner" \| "youtube-thumbnail" \| "medium-rectangle" \| "leaderboard" \| "wide-skyscraper" \| "mobile-banner" \| "half-page" \| "billboard" \| "iphone-15-screen" \| "desktop-1440" \| "4k-uhd" | no | Size preset (overrides width/height): open-graph-link-preview 1200×630, hero-16-9-full-hd 1920×1080, hd-16-9 1280×720, blog-image-4-3 800×600, thumbnail-3-2 600×400, avatar 256×256, instagram-square 1080×1080, instagram-portrait-4-5 1080×1350, story-reel-9-16 1080×1920, x-twitter-header 1500×500, linkedin-banner 1584×396, youtube-thumbnail 1280×720, medium-rectangle 300×250, leaderboard 728×90, wide-skyscraper 160×600, mobile-banner 320×50, half-page 300×600, billboard 970×250, iphone-15-screen 1179×2556, desktop-1440 1440×900, 4k-uhd 3840×2160. |
| `background` | string | no (default `"#1f2937"`) | Background colour (#rgb or #rrggbb). Default #1f2937. |
| `color` | string | no (default `"#9ca3af"`) | Text colour (#rgb or #rrggbb). Default #9ca3af. |
| `text` | string | no (default `""`) | Text to show. Empty = "W × H". Use "\n" (backslash n) or a real newline for a line break. |
| `fontSize` | number | no (default `0`) | Font size in px; 0 = auto-fit (default). |
| `fontWeight` | "400" \| "500" \| "600" \| "700" \| "800" \| "900" | no (default `"600"`) | Font weight. Default 600. |
| `format` | "png" \| "jpeg" \| "webp" \| "svg" | no (default `"png"`) | Output format. Default png. |
| `dataUri` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Also return the image as a data URI and an <img> tag (for small images). |
| `output` | string | no | Where to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set). |
| `returnBase64` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `path` | string |  |
| `bytes` | number |  |
| `mimeType` | string |  |
| `base64` | string |  |
| `width` | number |  |
| `height` | number |  |
| `format` | string |  |
| `aspectRatio` | string | Reduced ratio, e.g. "40:21". |
| `megapixels` | string |  |
| `fontSize` | number | Font size used, in px. |
| `dataUri` | string |  |
| `imgTag` | string |  |

**Example** — placeholder-1200x630.png with "1200 × 630".

```json
{
  "width": 1200,
  "height": 630
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "path": "/tmp/s3eidat-tools/placeholder-1200x630.png",
  "bytes": 28304,
  "mimeType": "image/png",
  "width": 1200,
  "height": 630,
  "format": "png",
  "aspectRatio": "40:21",
  "megapixels": "0.76",
  "fontSize": 135
}
```

**Example**

```json
{
  "preset": "leaderboard",
  "background": "#0e9f9a",
  "color": "#e6fffd",
  "text": "Ad space",
  "format": "svg"
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "path": "/tmp/s3eidat-tools/placeholder-728x90.svg",
  "bytes": 421,
  "mimeType": "image/svg+xml",
  "width": 728,
  "height": 90,
  "format": "svg",
  "aspectRatio": "364:45",
  "megapixels": "0.07",
  "fontSize": 31
}
```

<a id="image_resize"></a>
### `image_resize` — Resize image

Resize an image by pixels or percent, keeping the aspect ratio. Give width or height (the other side follows), or both: fit "cover" (default, as on the website) centre-crops to that ratio first so nothing is stretched, "contain" fits inside the box, "fill" stretches. Optional crop ratio (1:1, 4:5, 3:2, 16:9, 9:16, orig) or crop box before resizing, social-media presets, output JPEG/PNG/WebP/AVIF. Up to 16384 px a side. Output name: <name>-<W>x<H>.<ext>. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/image-resize/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `image` | string | yes | The image: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI). |
| `width` | integer | no | Target width in pixels. |
| `height` | integer | no | Target height in pixels. |
| `percent` | number | no | Scale by percent instead (e.g. 50 halves both sides, 200 doubles). |
| `preset` | "instagram-post" \| "instagram-portrait" \| "story" \| "youtube-thumbnail" \| "x-header" \| "facebook-cover" \| "linkedin-banner" \| "full-hd" \| "passport-35x45" \| "us-passport-2x2" | no | Exact output size preset: instagram-post 1080×1080, instagram-portrait 1080×1350, story 1080×1920, youtube-thumbnail 1280×720, x-header 1500×500, facebook-cover 851×315, linkedin-banner 1584×396, full-hd 1920×1080, passport-35x45 413×531, us-passport-2x2 600×600. |
| `fit` | "cover" \| "contain" \| "fill" | no (default `"cover"`) | When both width and height are set: cover (crop to fill, default), contain (fit inside), fill (stretch). |
| `ratio` | string \| number | no | Aspect ratio as "W:H" (e.g. "1:1", "4:5", "3:2", "16:9", "9:16"), a number (width/height), "orig" or "free". |
| `crop` | object | no | Crop box (x, y, width, height in pixels) applied before resizing; overrides ratio. |
| `format` | "keep" \| "jpeg" \| "png" \| "webp" \| "avif" | no (default `"keep"`) | Output format. "keep" = same as the input when it is JPEG/PNG/WebP (else PNG). Default keep. |
| `quality` | integer | no (default `90`) | Quality 1-100 for JPEG / WebP / AVIF (ignored for PNG). Default 90, same as the website. |
| `output` | string | no | Where to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set). |
| `returnBase64` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `path` | string | Absolute path of the written file. |
| `bytes` | number | Size of the written file in bytes. |
| `mimeType` | string |  |
| `base64` | string |  |
| `width` | number | Output width in pixels. |
| `height` | number | Output height in pixels. |
| `format` | string | Output format. |
| `crop` | object | Region of the input that was used. |

**Example** — Keeps the aspect ratio: 1600×900 → 1080×608.

```json
{
  "image": "/photos/beach.jpg",
  "width": 1080
}
```

**Example** — Centre-crops to 1:1 and writes beach-1080x1080.jpg.

```json
{
  "image": "/photos/beach.jpg",
  "preset": "instagram-post"
}
```

**Example**

```json
{
  "image": "/photos/beach.jpg",
  "percent": 50,
  "format": "webp"
}
```

<a id="image_rotate"></a>
### `image_rotate` — Rotate / flip image

Rotate an image clockwise by 90, 180, 270 or any angle (the canvas grows to fit; new corners get `background`), and/or flip it horizontally (mirror) or vertically. Flips apply after the rotation, as seen on screen. EXIF orientation is applied first (autoOrient, default on), so with angle 0 this simply "fixes" sideways phone photos. Output JPEG/PNG/WebP/AVIF ("keep" = same as input), name <name>-rotated.<ext>. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/photo-filters/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `image` | string | yes | The image: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI). |
| `angle` | number | no (default `90`) | Clockwise rotation in degrees (negative = counter-clockwise). Default 90. |
| `flipHorizontal` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Mirror left-right (after rotating). |
| `flipVertical` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Mirror top-bottom (after rotating). |
| `autoOrient` | boolean | no (default `true`) | Apply the EXIF Orientation tag first (what browsers show). Default true. |
| `background` | string | no (default `"#00000000"`) | Fill for the corners uncovered by a non-right-angle rotation. Default transparent (white in JPEG). |
| `format` | "keep" \| "jpeg" \| "png" \| "webp" \| "avif" | no (default `"keep"`) | Output format. Default keep (JPEG/PNG/WebP stay, others become PNG). |
| `quality` | integer | no (default `92`) | Quality 1-100 for JPEG / WebP / AVIF (ignored for PNG). Default 92, same as the website. |
| `output` | string | no | Where to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set). |
| `returnBase64` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `path` | string | Absolute path of the written file. |
| `bytes` | number | Size of the written file in bytes. |
| `mimeType` | string |  |
| `base64` | string |  |
| `width` | number | Output width in pixels. |
| `height` | number | Output height in pixels. |
| `format` | string | Output format. |
| `orientationApplied` | number | EXIF orientation that was applied (1 = none). |

**Example**

```json
{
  "image": "/photos/scan.jpg",
  "angle": 90
}
```

**Example** — Un-mirror a selfie.

```json
{
  "image": "/photos/selfie.jpg",
  "angle": 0,
  "flipHorizontal": true
}
```

**Example** — Bake the EXIF rotation into the pixels.

```json
{
  "image": "/photos/IMG_1234.jpg",
  "angle": 0
}
```

<a id="image_svg_to_png"></a>
### `image_svg_to_png` — SVG to PNG

Rasterise an SVG to a crisp PNG: scale (1×, 2×, 3×, 4×… default 2×), a target width or height (aspect kept), or an exact size (stretched). Units (px, pt, mm, cm, in) and viewBox-only SVGs are understood; transparent background or a solid colour. Scripts, event handlers and external references are removed first. Name <name>@2x.png. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/svg-to-png/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `svg` | string | no | The SVG: a local path or base64 / data URI. |
| `svgText` | string | no | Or: the SVG code itself. |
| `mode` | "scale" \| "width" \| "height" \| "exact" | no (default `"scale"`) | How to size the PNG. Default scale. |
| `scale` | number | no (default `2`) | Scale factor for mode "scale". Default 2. |
| `width` | integer | no | Width in px for modes width / exact. |
| `height` | integer | no | Height in px for modes height / exact. |
| `transparent` | boolean | no (default `true`) | Keep the background transparent. Default true. |
| `background` | string | no (default `"#ffffff"`) | Background colour when transparent is false. Default #ffffff. |
| `output` | string | no | Where to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set). |
| `returnBase64` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `path` | string |  |
| `bytes` | number |  |
| `mimeType` | string |  |
| `base64` | string |  |
| `width` | number |  |
| `height` | number |  |
| `svgWidth` | number | Natural width of the SVG. |
| `svgHeight` | number | Natural height of the SVG. |

**Example** — Writes logo@3x.png.

```json
{
  "svg": "/icons/logo.svg",
  "scale": 3
}
```

**Example** — 200×100 PNG.

```json
{
  "svgText": "<svg xmlns=\"http://www.w3.org/2000/svg\" viewBox=\"0 0 100 50\"/>",
  "mode": "width",
  "width": 200
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "path": "/tmp/s3eidat-tools/image.png",
  "bytes": 179,
  "mimeType": "image/png",
  "width": 200,
  "height": 100,
  "svgWidth": 100,
  "svgHeight": 50
}
```

<a id="image_to_base64"></a>
### `image_to_base64` — Image to Base64 / data URI

Encode an image (or any file) as Base64: a data URI, bare Base64, a CSS background-image rule, an HTML <img> tag (with width/height), Markdown, or for SVG a URL-encoded data URI (usually ~30% smaller). Detects the type from the bytes and warns when the result is too big to inline (over 10 KB: slows pages; over 1 MB: use a normal file). Results longer than maxChars are written to a .txt file instead of being returned. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/image-to-base64/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `image` | string | yes | The image: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI). |
| `as` | "dataUri" \| "base64" \| "css" \| "html" \| "markdown" \| "svgUri" | no (default `"dataUri"`) | What to return: dataUri (default), base64, css, html, markdown, or svgUri (SVG only). |
| `mimeType` | string | no | Override the detected MIME type (e.g. image/svg+xml). |
| `maxChars` | integer | no (default `200000`) | Longest text returned inline; longer results go to a file. Default 200000. |
| `output` | string | no | Where to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set). |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `text` | string | The requested snippet (omitted when written to a file). |
| `path` | string | Text file holding the snippet, when written to a file. |
| `mimeType` | string |  |
| `width` | number |  |
| `height` | number |  |
| `fileBytes` | number | Size of the original file. |
| `dataUriLength` | number | Length of the Base64 data URI in characters. |
| `overheadPercent` | number | How much bigger the data URI is than the file, in %. |
| `textLength` | number |  |
| `warning` | object |  |

**Example** — background-image: url("data:image/png;base64,…");

```json
{
  "image": "/site/icons/logo.png",
  "as": "css"
}
```

**Example**

```json
{
  "image": "/site/icons/arrow.svg",
  "as": "svgUri"
}
```

<a id="image_watermark"></a>
### `image_watermark` — Watermark image

Add a text or logo watermark to a photo. Size is a % of the photo width, margin a % of the shorter side, so the result looks the same on any resolution. One of 9 positions or tiled across the whole image (brick pattern), with opacity, rotation, colour, weight and a soft shadow for text. Logo: any image (PNG with transparency works best). Output keeps the input format (JPEG/PNG/WebP) or as chosen; name <name>-watermarked.<ext>. Text uses the Inter font when installed, else a system sans-serif. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/watermark/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `image` | string | yes | The photo: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI). |
| `text` | string | no (default `"© Your Name"`) | Watermark text (ignored when a logo is given). Default "© Your Name". |
| `logo` | string | no | Use this image as the watermark instead of text: a local path or base64. |
| `color` | string | no (default `"#ffffff"`) | Text colour. Default #ffffff. |
| `weight` | "400" \| "500" \| "600" \| "700" \| "800" \| "900" | no (default `"700"`) | Font weight. Default 700. |
| `shadow` | boolean | no (default `true`) | Soft drop shadow behind text. Default true. |
| `position` | "top-left" \| "top" \| "top-right" \| "left" \| "center" \| "right" \| "bottom-left" \| "bottom" \| "bottom-right" | no (default `"bottom-right"`) | Where the watermark sits (ignored when tiled). Default bottom-right. |
| `tiled` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Repeat the watermark across the whole photo. |
| `opacity` | number | no (default `60`) | Opacity in %. Default 60. |
| `size` | number | no (default `28`) | Watermark width as % of the photo width. Default 28. |
| `rotation` | number | no (default `0`) | Rotation in degrees (e.g. -30 for a diagonal tile). Default 0. |
| `margin` | number | no (default `4`) | Margin from the edge in % of the shorter side; spacing between tiles when tiled. Default 4. |
| `format` | "keep" \| "jpeg" \| "png" \| "webp" | no (default `"keep"`) | Output format. Default keep. |
| `quality` | integer | no (default `92`) | Quality 1-100 for JPEG / WebP / AVIF (ignored for PNG). Default 92, same as the website. |
| `output` | string | no | Where to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set). |
| `returnBase64` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `path` | string | Absolute path of the written file. |
| `bytes` | number | Size of the written file in bytes. |
| `mimeType` | string |  |
| `base64` | string |  |
| `width` | number | Output width in pixels. |
| `height` | number | Output height in pixels. |
| `format` | string | Output format. |

**Example**

```json
{
  "image": "/photos/shoot-01.jpg",
  "text": "© Studio Lina",
  "position": "bottom-right",
  "opacity": 60
}
```

**Example**

```json
{
  "image": "/photos/proof.jpg",
  "text": "PROOF",
  "tiled": true,
  "rotation": -25,
  "opacity": 35,
  "size": 26,
  "margin": 3
}
```

**Example**

```json
{
  "image": "/photos/product.png",
  "logo": "/brand/logo.png",
  "position": "top-left",
  "size": 20
}
```

<a id="text"></a>
## Text

Counting, case, slugs, cleaning, diffs, readability, Arabic tools, numbers to words, citations.

| Tool | What it does |
|---|---|
| [`text_arabic`](#text_arabic) | Arabic text tools |
| [`text_case`](#text_case) | Case convert |
| [`text_citation`](#text_citation) | Citation generator |
| [`text_clean`](#text_clean) | Text cleaner |
| [`text_diff`](#text_diff) | Diff two texts |
| [`text_escape`](#text_escape) | Escape / unescape text |
| [`text_fancy`](#text_fancy) | Fancy text (Unicode styles) |
| [`text_layout_fix`](#text_layout_fix) | Keyboard layout fixer (EN ⇄ AR) |
| [`text_lorem`](#text_lorem) | Lorem ipsum generator |
| [`text_morse`](#text_morse) | Morse code translator |
| [`text_number_to_words`](#text_number_to_words) | Number to words (English & Arabic) |
| [`text_readability`](#text_readability) | Readability score |
| [`text_slugify`](#text_slugify) | Slugify |
| [`text_social_count`](#text_social_count) | Social media character counter |
| [`text_word_count`](#text_word_count) | Word count |

<a id="text_arabic"></a>
### `text_arabic` — Arabic text tools

Arabic text utilities. mode "clean": remove tashkeel (harakat and Quranic marks, optionally keeping shadda), remove tatweel (kashida), normalise letters (alef with hamza/madda/wasla → bare alef; alef maqsura and Farsi ya → ya; ta marbuta → ha; hamza on waw/ya → waw/ya), switch Latin , ; ? to Arabic punctuation, and convert digits (Arabic-Indic ↔ Western). mode "arabizi": turn Arabizi / Franco-Arabic chat text (mar7aba, 3arabi, 5ala9, el bait) into Arabic script. Returns the result with letter/word/character counts. Give "text" or a text file "path". Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/arabic-tools/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `text` | string | no | The text to transform. |
| `path` | string | no | Or: a path to a UTF-8 text file. |
| `mode` | "clean" \| "arabizi" | no (default `"clean"`) | "clean" = clean & normalise Arabic text (default); "arabizi" = Arabizi (Latin letters + numbers) → Arabic script. |
| `removeTashkeel` | boolean | no (default `true`) | clean: remove tashkeel / harakat (default true). |
| `keepShadda` | boolean | no (default `false`) | clean: when removing tashkeel, keep the shadda. |
| `removeTatweel` | boolean | no (default `true`) | clean: remove tatweel / kashida (default true). |
| `normalizeAlef` | boolean | no (default `false`) | clean: alef with hamza above/below, madda or wasla → bare alef. |
| `normalizeYa` | boolean | no (default `false`) | clean: alef maqsura and Farsi ya → Arabic ya. |
| `taMarbutaToHa` | boolean | no (default `false`) | clean: ta marbuta → ha. |
| `normalizeHamza` | boolean | no (default `false`) | clean: hamza on waw → waw, hamza on ya → ya. |
| `arabicPunctuation` | boolean | no (default `false`) | clean: Latin comma, semicolon and question mark → Arabic ones. |
| `digits` | "keep" \| "western" \| "arabic" | no (default `"keep"`) | clean: keep digits, convert Arabic-Indic/Persian digits to 0-9 ("western"), or 0-9 to Arabic-Indic ("arabic"). |
| `output` | string | no | Optional: write the result to this file path (or folder ending in "/") instead of returning it. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `text` | string | The result (omitted when written to "output"). |
| `path` | string |  |
| `bytes` | number |  |
| `arabicLetters` | number |  |
| `words` | number |  |
| `characters` | number |  |
| `marksRemoved` | number | clean mode: how many tashkeel marks were removed. |

**Example**

```json
{
  "mode": "arabizi",
  "text": "mar7aba, kifak?"
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "text": "مرحبا، كيفك؟",
  "arabicLetters": 9,
  "words": 2,
  "characters": 12
}
```

**Example**

```json
{
  "text": "2026",
  "digits": "arabic"
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "text": "٢٠٢٦",
  "arabicLetters": 0,
  "words": 1,
  "characters": 4,
  "marksRemoved": 0
}
```

<a id="text_case"></a>
### `text_case` — Case convert

Convert text case: upper, lower, title (smart: keeps small words lower and iPhone/NASA as typed), sentence, camel, pascal, snake, kebab, constant, alternate, inverse. Works line by line and handles Unicode (Arabic, accents, ß). Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/case-convert/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `text` | string | yes | Text to convert. |
| `mode` | "upper" \| "lower" \| "title" \| "sentence" \| "camel" \| "pascal" \| "snake" \| "kebab" \| "constant" \| "alternate" \| "inverse" | yes | Target case. |

**Output**
