| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `result` | string |  |

**Example**

```json
{
  "text": "a tale OF two cities",
  "mode": "title"
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "result": "A Tale of Two Cities"
}
```

**Example**

```json
{
  "text": "XMLParser handler",
  "mode": "snake"
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "result": "xml_parser_handler"
}
```

<a id="text_citation"></a>
### `text_citation` — Citation generator

Format references from structured fields in APA 7, MLA 9, Chicago author-date (17th), Harvard (Cite Them Right) or IEEE, for books, book chapters, journal articles, web pages, newspaper/magazine articles, videos and reports. Handles author-count rules (et al.), initials, organisations as authors, editions, page ranges (MLA 127-53), DOIs, URLs and access dates, and title case for MLA/Chicago/IEEE. Returns each citation as plain text and HTML (italics as <i>), the in-text citation, missing-field hints, and the whole list as a bibliography sorted for the style (numbered for IEEE). Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/citation-gen/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `style` | "apa" \| "mla" \| "chicago" \| "harvard" \| "ieee" | yes | Citation style: apa, mla, chicago, harvard or ieee. |
| `sources` | object[] | yes | One or more sources. |
| `autoCase` | boolean | no (default `true`) | Apply title case where the style wants it (MLA, Chicago, IEEE book titles). Default true. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `style` | string |  |
| `citations` | object[] | In the order given. |
| `bibliographyTitle` | string | References / Works Cited / Reference list. |
| `bibliography` | string | All entries sorted for the style (IEEE: numbered in the order given), separated by blank lines. |

**Example** — Abelson, H., & Sussman, G. J. (1996). Structure and interpretation of computer programs (2nd ed.). MIT Press.

```json
{
  "style": "apa",
  "sources": [
    {
      "type": "book",
      "authors": [
        {
          "first": "Harold",
          "last": "Abelson"
        },
        {
          "first": "Gerald Jay",
          "last": "Sussman"
        }
      ],
      "title": "Structure and interpretation of computer programs",
      "edition": "2",
      "year": "1996",
      "publisher": "MIT Press",
      "place": "Cambridge, MA, USA"
    }
  ]
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "style": "APA 7",
  "citations": [
    {
      "text": "Abelson, H., & Sussman, G. J. (1996). Structure and interpretation of computer programs (2nd ed.). MIT Press.",
      "html": "Abelson, H., &amp; Sussman, G. J. (1996). <i>Structure and interpretation of computer programs</i> (2nd ed.). MIT Press.",
      "inText": "(Abelson & Sussman, 1996)",
      "missing": []
    }
  ],
  "bibliographyTitle": "References",
  "bibliography": "Abelson, H., & Sussman, G. J. (1996). Structure and interpretation of computer programs (2nd ed.). MIT Press."
}
```

<a id="text_clean"></a>
### `text_clean` — Text cleaner

Clean up text line by line with a fixed pipeline (same order as the website): strip HTML tags and decode entities → remove emojis → trim each line → collapse repeated spaces → remove empty lines → remove duplicate lines (optionally ignoring case) → sort (A–Z, Z–A, natural, by length) → reverse → shuffle (seedable) → add line numbers. Returns the cleaned text plus counts, or writes it to a file when "output" is given. Give "text" or a text file "path". Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/text-cleaner/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `text` | string | no | The text to clean. |
| `path` | string | no | Or: a path to a UTF-8 text file. |
| `stripHtml` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Strip HTML tags (drops script/style/comments, block ends become line breaks) and decode entities (default false). |
| `removeEmoji` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Remove emojis (©, ® and ™ are kept) (default false). |
| `trim` | boolean | no (default `true`) | Trim spaces at the start and end of each line (default true). |
| `collapseSpaces` | boolean | no (default `true`) | Collapse runs of spaces/tabs into one space (default true). |
| `removeEmptyLines` | boolean | no (default `true`) | Remove empty (whitespace-only) lines (default true). |
| `removeDuplicates` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Remove duplicate lines, keeping the first (default false). |
| `ignoreCase` | boolean | no (default `false`) | When removing duplicates, treat lines that differ only in case as duplicates (default false). |
| `sort` | "none" \| "az" \| "za" \| "natural" \| "length" | no (default `"none"`) | Sort lines: none (default), az, za, natural (item 2 before item 10), length (shortest first). |
| `reverse` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Reverse the line order (default false). |
| `shuffle` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Shuffle the lines (default false). |
| `seed` | integer | no | Seed for a reproducible shuffle (default: random). |
| `numberLines` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Prefix each line with its number ("1. ", padded) (default false). |
| `output` | string | no | Optional: write the cleaned text to this file path (or folder ending in "/") instead of returning it. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `text` | string | The cleaned text (omitted when written to "output"). |
| `path` | string |  |
| `bytes` | number |  |
| `linesIn` | number |  |
| `linesOut` | number |  |
| `duplicatesRemoved` | number |  |
| `emptyLinesRemoved` | number |  |
| `charactersOut` | number |  |
| `seed` | number | Seed used for the shuffle. |

**Example**

```json
{
  "text": "item 10\nitem 2\nitem 2\n\nitem 1",
  "removeDuplicates": true,
  "sort": "natural"
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "text": "item 1\nitem 2\nitem 10",
  "linesIn": 5,
  "linesOut": 3,
  "duplicatesRemoved": 1,
  "emptyLinesRemoved": 1,
  "charactersOut": 21
}
```

<a id="text_diff"></a>
### `text_diff` — Diff two texts

Compare two texts line by line (Myers diff) and return a unified diff (3 lines of context, like `diff -u`), counts of added/removed/unchanged lines, and change blocks where each removed line is paired with the added line that replaced it and the changed words are marked [-old-] / {+new+}. Can ignore whitespace and case. Works with any language (Arabic included). Up to 50,000 lines per side. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/diff-view/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `original` | string | no | The original text ("before"). |
| `originalPath` | string | no | Or: path to the original text file. |
| `changed` | string | no | The changed text ("after"). |
| `changedPath` | string | no | Or: path to the changed text file. |
| `ignoreWhitespace` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Treat lines that differ only in spacing as equal. |
| `ignoreCase` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Treat lines that differ only in letter case as equal. |
| `wordDiff` | boolean | no (default `true`) | Mark the changed words inside paired lines (default true). |
| `nameA` | string | no | Label for the "---" header (default "original"). |
| `nameB` | string | no | Label for the "+++" header (default "changed"). |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `identical` | boolean | True when there is no difference (with the ignore options applied). |
| `added` | number |  |
| `removed` | number |  |
| `unchanged` | number |  |
| `unified` | string | Unified diff text (empty when identical). |
| `changes` | object[] | Change blocks; line numbers are 1-based. |

**Example**

```json
{
  "original": "one\ntwo\nthree",
  "changed": "zero\none\nthree"
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "identical": false,
  "added": 1,
  "removed": 1,
  "unchanged": 2,
  "unified": "--- original\n+++ changed\n@@ -1,3 +1,3 @@\n+zero\n one\n-two\n three\n",
  "changes": [
    {
      "removed": [],
      "added": [
        {
          "line": 1,
          "text": "zero"
        }
      ]
    },
    {
      "removed": [
        {
          "line": 2,
          "text": "two"
        }
      ],
      "added": []
    }
  ]
}
```

**Example** — changes[0]: removed marked "the [-quick-] fox", added marked "the {+slow+} fox"

```json
{
  "original": "the quick fox",
  "changed": "the slow fox"
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "identical": false,
  "added": 1,
  "removed": 1,
  "unchanged": 0,
  "unified": "--- original\n+++ changed\n@@ -1 +1 @@\n-the quick fox\n+the slow fox\n",
  "changes": [
    {
      "removed": [
        {
          "line": 1,
          "text": "the quick fox",
          "marked": "the [-quick-] fox"
        }
      ],
      "added": [
        {
          "line": 1,
          "text": "the slow fox",
          "marked": "the {+slow+} fox"
        }
      ]
    }
  ]
}
```

<a id="text_escape"></a>
### `text_escape` — Escape / unescape text

Escape or unescape a string for a target: JSON (string body), JavaScript (incl. template literals), HTML, XML, URL (encodeURIComponent, optional form +), SQL (doubled quotes, optional MySQL backslashes and wrapping quotes), CSV field (RFC 4180; comma, semicolon or tab), regex, POSIX shell single quotes, C/C++, Java/C#, and Unicode escapes (\uXXXX, \u{X}, \UXXXXXXXX, &#xX;, U+XXXX). Options per target as on the website. Set "allTargets" to get the escaped form for every target at once. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/text-escape/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `text` | string | yes | The text to escape, or the escaped text to unescape. |
| `target` | "json" \| "js" \| "html" \| "xml" \| "url" \| "sql" \| "csv" \| "regex" \| "shell" \| "c" \| "java" \| "unicode" | yes | Format: json, js, html, xml, url, sql, csv, regex, shell, c, java, unicode. |
| `direction` | "escape" \| "unescape" | no (default `"escape"`) | escape (default) or unescape. |
| `ascii` | boolean | no (default `false`) | json/js/html/c/java: also escape non-ASCII characters (c: as UTF-8 octal). |
| `named` | boolean | no (default `false`) | html (with ascii): prefer named entities such as &copy;. |
| `form` | boolean | no (default `false`) | url: form style, + for spaces. |
| `wrap` | boolean | no (default `false`) | sql: wrap in single quotes (unescape: strip them). |
| `mysql` | boolean | no (default `false`) | sql: escape backslashes too (MySQL). |
| `alwaysQuote` | boolean | no (default `false`) | csv: always quote the field. |
| `delimiter` | "," \| ";" \| "\t" | no (default `","`) | csv: field delimiter (comma, semicolon or tab). |
| `unicodeFormat` | "u4" \| "ubrace" \| "u8" \| "html" \| "uplus" | no (default `"u4"`) | unicode: u4 = \uXXXX (JS, JSON, Java), ubrace = \u{X} (ES6, Rust, Swift), u8 = \UXXXXXXXX (Python, C), html = &#xX;, uplus = U+XXXX. |
| `every` | boolean | no (default `false`) | unicode: escape every character, not only non-ASCII. |
| `allTargets` | boolean | no (default `false`) | escape only: also return the text escaped for every target. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `result` | string |  |
| `note` | string | A short hint about the target. |
| `inputCharacters` | number |  |
| `outputCharacters` | number |  |
| `allTargets` | object[] |  |

**Example**

```json
{
  "text": "O'Brien",
  "target": "sql",
  "wrap": true
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "result": "'O''Brien'",
  "note": "Standard SQL doubles single quotes. Prefer parameterised queries over escaping.",
  "inputCharacters": 7,
  "outputCharacters": 10
}
```

**Example**

```json
{
  "text": "a%20b%26c",
  "target": "url",
  "direction": "unescape"
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "result": "a b&c",
  "note": "encodeURIComponent: for one query value or path segment.",
  "inputCharacters": 9,
  "outputCharacters": 5
}
```

<a id="text_fancy"></a>
### `text_fancy` — Fancy text (Unicode styles)

Restyle text with Unicode look-alike characters for bios and posts (28 styles): Bold, Italic, Bold italic, Script, Bold script, Fraktur, Bold fraktur, Double-struck, Sans-serif, Sans bold, Sans italic, Sans bold italic, Monospace, Circled, Circled dark, Squared, Squared dark, Parenthesized, Fullwidth, Small caps, Superscript, Upside down, Strikethrough, Underline, Double underline, Slashed, Overline, Wide spaced. Only Latin letters and digits change (other scripts such as Arabic and emoji pass through); some styles cover only capitals or only some symbols. Give one "style", or none to get every style. Up to 20,000 characters. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/fancy-text/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `text` | string | yes | The text to restyle. |
| `style` | "Bold" \| "Italic" \| "Bold italic" \| "Script" \| "Bold script" \| "Fraktur" \| "Bold fraktur" \| "Double-struck" \| "Sans-serif" \| "Sans bold" \| "Sans italic" \| "Sans bold italic" \| "Monospace" \| "Circled" \| "Circled dark" \| "Squared" \| "Squared dark" \| "Parenthesized" \| "Fullwidth" \| "Small caps" \| "Superscript" \| "Upside down" \| "Strikethrough" \| "Underline" \| "Double underline" \| "Slashed" \| "Overline" \| "Wide spaced" | no | One style name (exact, e.g. "Bold", "Script", "Upside down"). Omit to get all styles. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `results` | object[] |  |

**Example**

```json
{
  "text": "Hello 2026",
  "style": "Bold"
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "results": [
    {
      "style": "Bold",
      "text": "𝐇𝐞𝐥𝐥𝐨 𝟐𝟎𝟐𝟔"
    }
  ]
}
```

**Example** — Returns all 28 styles.

```json
{
  "text": "Stay curious"
}
```

Result:

```json

  "results": [
    {
      "style": "Bold",
```

<a id="text_layout_fix"></a>
### `text_layout_fix` — Keyboard layout fixer (EN ⇄ AR)

Fix text typed with the wrong keyboard layout between US English (QWERTY) and Arabic (101): "hgsghl ugd;l" becomes the Arabic greeting, and Arabic gibberish typed on English keys becomes "hello". Direction "auto" detects it; in mixed text only the words that look mistyped are converted (using small English/Arabic letter-pair models), so words that are already right stay as they are. Handles the shift layer (harakat, hamza forms), lam-alef keys and Caps Lock (all-caps Latin input is treated as lowercase). Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/layout-fixer/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `text` | string | no | The mistyped text. |
| `path` | string | no | Or: a path to a UTF-8 text file. |
| `direction` | "auto" \| "ar" \| "en" | no (default `"auto"`) | "auto" (default) detects; "ar" = typed on English keys, convert to Arabic; "en" = typed on Arabic keys, convert to English. |
| `capsLock` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Caps Lock was on: treat capital Latin letters as lowercase (detected automatically when all letters are capitals). |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `text` | string | The fixed text. |
| `direction` | "ar" \| "en" \| "mixed" \| null | ar = converted to Arabic, en = converted to English, mixed = only mistyped words fixed, null = no letters. |
| `wordsChanged` | number |  |
| `capsLockIgnored` | boolean | True when all-caps input was treated as Caps Lock. |

**Example**

```json
{
  "text": ";dt phg;?"
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "text": "كيف حالك؟",
  "direction": "ar",
  "wordsChanged": 2,
  "capsLockIgnored": false
}
```

**Example** — Mixed text: only the mistyped Arabic-looking word becomes "hello".

```json
{
  "text": "Hi, ok اثممخ"
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "text": "Hi, ok hello",
  "direction": "mixed",
  "wordsChanged": 1,
  "capsLockIgnored": false
}
```

<a id="text_lorem"></a>
### `text_lorem` — Lorem ipsum generator

Generate placeholder text: classic Lorem ipsum, readable English filler, or Arabic filler. Choose paragraphs (max 100), sentences (max 500) or words (max 10000); optionally start with the classic opener and wrap paragraphs in <p> tags. Give a seed for repeatable output (the same seed gives the same text). Writes a .txt/.html file instead when "output" is given. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/lorem-gen/

Hints: readOnly

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `style` | "lorem" \| "english" \| "arabic" | no (default `"lorem"`) | Word list: lorem (Latin), english, or arabic. |
| `unit` | "paragraphs" \| "sentences" \| "words" | no (default `"paragraphs"`) | What "count" counts. |
| `count` | integer | no (default `3`) | How many paragraphs / sentences / words (clamped to the unit limit). |
| `opener` | boolean | no (default `true`) | Start with the classic opening line ("Lorem ipsum dolor sit amet…", "The quick brown fox…", or the Arabic one). Default true. |
| `html` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Wrap each paragraph in <p>…</p>. |
| `seed` | integer | no | Seed for repeatable output (default: random). |
| `output` | string | no | Optional: write the text to this file path (or folder ending in "/") instead of returning it. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `text` | string | The generated text (omitted when written to "output"). |
| `path` | string |  |
| `bytes` | number |  |
| `words` | number |  |
| `characters` | number |  |
| `paragraphs` | number |  |
| `seed` | number | Seed used: pass it again to get the same text. |

**Example** — 25 words starting "Lorem ipsum dolor sit amet…"

```json
{
  "unit": "words",
  "count": 25,
  "seed": 42
}
```

**Example**

```json
{
  "style": "arabic",
  "unit": "paragraphs",
  "count": 2
}
```

<a id="text_morse"></a>
### `text_morse` — Morse code translator

Translate text to International Morse code and back. Encoding: letters separated by spaces, words by " / "; accents are folded (é → E) and characters without a code are skipped and counted. Decoding accepts . and - plus look-alikes (• · – — _), with words split by /, | or 2+ spaces; unknown codes become #. Encoding also returns the playing time at a given speed (PARIS timing: dot = 1200/WPM ms). No audio. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/morse-code/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `text` | string | yes | Text to encode, or Morse code to decode. |
| `mode` | "encode" \| "decode" \| "auto" | no (default `"auto"`) | "encode" text → Morse, "decode" Morse → text, or "auto" (default): decode if the input only has Morse symbols, spaces, / and \|. |
| `wpm` | number | no (default `18`) | Speed in words per minute for the duration estimate (default 18). |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `mode` | "encode" \| "decode" |  |
| `result` | string |  |
| `skipped` | number | encode: characters with no Morse code (skipped). |
| `unrecognized` | number | decode: codes that were not recognised (shown as #). |
| `durationSeconds` | number | encode: time to play the Morse at the given WPM. |

**Example**

```json
{
  "text": "SOS",
  "mode": "encode"
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "mode": "encode",
  "result": "... --- ...",
  "skipped": 0,
  "durationSeconds": 1.8
}
```

**Example**

```json
{
  "text": ".... ..  /  - .... . .-. ."
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "mode": "decode",
  "result": "HI THERE",
  "unrecognized": 0
}
```

<a id="text_number_to_words"></a>
### `text_number_to_words` — Number to words (English & Arabic)

Spell a number in words in English and Arabic: English cardinal (with decimals, optional British "and"), ordinal (twenty-first), cheque wording for an amount in a currency (cents in words or as 25/100, optional "only"), Arabic cardinal, and Arabic tafqeet for amounts with correct gender/number agreement of the currency noun (optional "faqat … la ghayr" wrapper and the modern 100 spelling). Currencies: JOD, USD, SAR, AED, KWD, EGP, GBP (or none). Accepts Arabic-Indic digits and thousands separators. Up to 999 trillion (15 digits). Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/number-words/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `number` | string \| number | yes | The number or amount, e.g. "1500.250", "1,234,567.89", -12.05 (Arabic-Indic digits work too). |
| `currency` | "none" \| "JOD" \| "USD" \| "SAR" \| "AED" \| "KWD" \| "EGP" \| "GBP" | no (default `"none"`) | Currency for cheque / tafqeet wording: none (default), JOD, USD, SAR, AED, KWD, EGP, GBP. |
| `only` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Add "only" (English) and the Arabic "faqat … la ghayr" wrapper to amounts. |
| `british` | boolean | no (default `false`) | British English "and": one hundred and five. |
| `subStyle` | "words" \| "fraction" | no (default `"words"`) | Cheque sub-units in words (twenty-five cents) or as a fraction (25/100). |
| `modernHundred` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Arabic: use the modern spelling of "hundred" (without the extra alif). |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `digits` | string | The number as parsed, with thousands separators. |
| `english` | string | English cardinal words. |
| `ordinal` | string | English ordinal (whole non-negative numbers only). |
| `ordinalShort` | string | Ordinal in digits, e.g. 21st. |
| `arabic` | string | Arabic cardinal words. |
| `englishAmount` | string | Cheque wording in the currency (when a currency is set). |
| `arabicAmount` | string | Arabic tafqeet of the amount (when a currency is set). |

**Example**

```json
{
  "number": "21"
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "digits": "21",
  "english": "twenty-one",
  "ordinal": "twenty-first",
  "ordinalShort": "21st",
  "arabic": "واحد وعشرون"
}
```

**Example** — englishAmount: "One thousand five hundred dollars and twenty-five cents", plus the Arabic tafqeet

```json
{
  "number": "1500.25",
  "currency": "USD"
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "digits": "1,500.25 USD",
  "english": "one thousand five hundred point two five",
  "arabic": "ألف وخمسمائة فاصلة خمسة وعشرون",
  "englishAmount": "One thousand five hundred dollars and twenty-five cents",
  "arabicAmount": "ألف وخمسمائة دولار وخمسة وعشرون سنتاً"
}
```

<a id="text_readability"></a>
### `text_readability` — Readability score

Score how easy an English text is to read: Flesch Reading Ease, Flesch-Kincaid Grade, Gunning Fog, SMOG, Coleman-Liau and Automated Readability Index (same formulas, sentence splitter and syllable estimate as the website), plus a verdict, average grade level, and Hemingway-style hints: long sentences (over 20 / 30 words), passive voice and adverbs. The formulas are built for English; for Arabic text the result says so (arabicWarning). Give "text" or a text file "path". Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/readability/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `text` | string | no | The text to score. |
| `path` | string | no | Or: a path to a UTF-8 text file. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `readingEase` | number \| null | Flesch Reading Ease clamped to 0–100 and rounded (the big number on the website). |
| `verdict` | string \| null |  |
| `verdictLevel` | "ok" \| "warn" \| "bad" \| null |  |
| `advice` | string \| null |  |
| `averageGrade` | number \| null | Mean of the five grade-level formulas. |
| `averageGradeText` | string |  |
| `scores` | object \| null |  |
| `words` | number |  |
| `sentences` | number |  |
| `syllablesPerWord` | number \| null |  |
| `wordsPerSentence` | number \| null |  |
| `longSentences` | object[] | Sentences over 20 (hard) or 30 (vhard) words. |
| `passive` | string[] | Passive-voice phrases found. |
| `adverbs` | string[] | Adverbs found (-ly words, with common non-adverbs excluded). |
| `tips` | string[] |  |
| `smogNote` | string |  |
| `arabicWarning` | string |  |

**Example** — Returns all six scores, the verdict and hints.

```json
{
  "text": "Keep it short. Use plain words. Readers scan, so put the main point first and cut what they don't need."
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "readingEase": 100,
  "verdict": "Very easy",
  "verdictLevel": "ok",
  "advice": "Short sentences and everyday words. Almost anyone can follow it.",
  "averageGrade": 1.7,
  "averageGradeText": "grade 2 · age 7–8",
  "scores": {
    "fleschReadingEase": 111.2,
    "fleschKincaidGrade": {
      "score": -0.6,
      "grade": "kindergarten"
    },
    "gunningFog": {
      "score": 2.7,
      "grade": "grade 3 · age 8–9"
    },
    "smog": {
      "score": 3.1,
      "grade": "grade 3 · age 8–9"
    },
    "colemanLiau": {
      "score": 3,
      "grade": "grade 3 · age 8–9"
    },
    "automatedReadability": {
      "score": 0.5,
      "grade": "grade 1 · age 6–7"
    }
  },
  "words": 20,
  "sentences": 3,
  "syllablesPerWord": 1.05,
  "wordsPerSentence": 6.7,
  "longSentences": [],
  "passive": [],
  "adverbs": [],
  "tips": [
    "No long sentences, passive voice or extra adverbs found."
  ],
  "smogNote": "SMOG is designed for 30+ sentences; with 3 it is a rough estimate."
}
```

<a id="text_slugify"></a>
### `text_slugify` — Slugify

Turn titles into clean URL slugs, one slug per non-empty line (bulk). Folds accents (é → e, ß → ss), transliterates Arabic to Latin (the article al- becomes its own word) or keeps Arabic letters, converts Arabic-Indic digits, drops tashkeel, turns & into "and", can remove stop words (English and Arabic) and cap the length at a word boundary. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/slugify/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `text` | string | yes | Title(s) to slugify. Each non-empty line becomes one slug. |
| `separator` | "-" \| "_" \| "." | no (default `"-"`) | Word separator: dash (default), underscore or dot. |
| `lowercase` | boolean | no (default `true`) | Lowercase the slug (default true). |
| `foldAccents` | boolean | no (default `true`) | Transliterate accents: é → e, ß → ss, æ → ae (default true). |
| `removeStopWords` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Remove stop words in English and Arabic such as the, of, and (kept if nothing else is left). |
| `arabic` | "latin" \| "keep" | no (default `"latin"`) | "latin" transliterates Arabic letters (default); "keep" keeps them. |
| `maxLength` | integer | no (default `0`) | Maximum slug length in characters, cut at a word boundary (0 = no limit). |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `slugs` | object[] |  |

**Example**

```json
{
  "text": "Crème Brûlée & Café au Lait: A Love Story"
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "slugs": [
    {
      "title": "Crème Brûlée & Café au Lait: A Love Story",
      "slug": "creme-brulee-and-cafe-au-lait-a-love-story",
      "length": 42,
      "encodedLength": 42
    }
  ]
}
```

**Example** — lord-rings

```json
{
  "text": "The Lord of the Rings",
  "removeStopWords": true
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "slugs": [
    {
      "title": "The Lord of the Rings",
      "slug": "lord-rings",
      "length": 10,
      "encodedLength": 10
    }
  ]
}
```

<a id="text_social_count"></a>
### `text_social_count` — Social media character counter

Check a post against platform limits the way the platforms count: X/Twitter weighted length (links = 23, emoji = 2, CJK = 2; limit 280), Threads 500, Bluesky 300, Instagram 2,200 (+ 30 hashtags), LinkedIn 3,000, Facebook 63,206, TikTok 4,000, YouTube title 100 / description 5,000, Pinterest 500, Google title ~60, meta description ~160. Characters are counted as user-perceived graphemes (one emoji = 1). Also: words, hashtags, mentions, links, emoji, and SMS encoding (GSM-7 vs Unicode) with the number of SMS parts and the characters that force Unicode. Give "text" or a text file "path". Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/social-counter/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `text` | string | no | The post text. |
| `path` | string | no | Or: a path to a UTF-8 text file. |
| `platforms` | ("x" \| "threads" \| "bsky" \| "ig" \| "li" \| "fb" \| "tt" \| "yt" \| "ytd" \| "pin" \| "gt" \| "gd")[] | no | Only these meters: x, threads, bsky, ig, li, fb, tt, yt, ytd, pin, gt (Google title), gd (meta description). Default: all. |
| `gsmLookalikes` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Also return the text with curly quotes, dashes, ellipses, odd spaces etc. replaced by GSM-7 look-alikes (cheaper SMS). |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `characters` | number |  |
| `words` | number |  |
| `hashtags` | number |  |
| `mentions` | number |  |
| `links` | number |  |
| `emoji` | number |  |
| `lines` | number |  |
| `utf16Units` | number |  |
| `xWeightedLength` | number |  |
| `platforms` | object[] |  |
| `sms` | object |  |
| `gsmText` | string |  |

**Example** — xWeightedLength 48: the link counts 23 and the emoji 2.

```json
{
  "text": "Read it here: https://example.com/blog/code-reviews 🚀 #coding",
  "platforms": [
    "x"
  ]
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "characters": 61,
  "words": 9,
  "hashtags": 1,
  "mentions": 0,
  "links": 1,
  "emoji": 1,
  "lines": 1,
  "utf16Units": 62,
  "xWeightedLength": 48,
  "platforms": [
    {
      "id": "x",
      "name": "X (Twitter) post",
      "count": 48,
      "limit": 280,
      "softLimit": false,
      "remaining": 232,
      "over": 0,
      "status": "ok",
      "notes": [
        "Weighted: links 23, emoji 2, CJK 2. Premium allows 25,000."
      ]
    }
  ],
  "sms": {
    "encoding": "UCS-2",
    "parts": 1,
    "unitsUsed": 62,
    "perPart": 70,
    "leftInPart": 8,
    "nonGsmCharacters": [
      {
        "char": "🚀",
        "count": 1
      }
    ]
  }
}
```

<a id="text_word_count"></a>
### `text_word_count` — Word count

Count words, characters (with and without spaces), sentences, paragraphs, lines and unique words; estimate reading (238 wpm) and speaking (150 wpm) time; list the most used keywords with density. Works for English and Arabic (tashkeel ignored). Give "text" or a text file "path". Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/word-count/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `text` | string | no | The text to analyse. |
| `path` | string | no | Or: a path to a UTF-8 text file. |
| `keywords` | integer | no (default `12`) | How many top keywords to return (0 = none). |
| `includeStopWords` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Count common words like "the"/"في" as keywords. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `words` | number |  |
| `characters` | number |  |
| `charactersNoSpaces` | number |  |
| `sentences` | number |  |
| `paragraphs` | number |  |
| `lines` | number |  |
| `uniqueWords` | number |  |
| `averageWordLength` | number |  |
| `longestWord` | string |  |
| `readingTimeSeconds` | number |  |
| `speakingTimeSeconds` | number |  |
| `keywords` | object[] |  |

**Example** — 11 words, 4 sentences, 2 paragraphs

```json
{
  "text": "Hello world. This is a test! Is it?\n\nNew paragraph here."
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "words": 11,
  "characters": 56,
  "charactersNoSpaces": 45,
  "sentences": 4,
  "paragraphs": 2,
  "lines": 3,
  "uniqueWords": 10,
  "averageWordLength": 3.73,
  "longestWord": "paragraph",
  "readingTimeSeconds": 3,
  "speakingTimeSeconds": 4,
  "keywords": [
    {
      "word": "hello",
      "count": 1,
      "density": 9.09
    },
    {
      "word": "new",
      "count": 1,
      "density": 9.09
    },
    {
      "word": "paragraph",
      "count": 1,
      "density": 9.09
    },
    {
      "word": "test",
      "count": 1,
      "density": 9.09
    },
    {
      "word": "world",
      "count": 1,
      "density": 9.09
    }
  ]
}
```

JSON, CSV, YAML, XML, SQL, HTML/CSS formatting and conversion, types from JSON, curl to code.

| [`csv_to_json`](#csv_to_json) | CSV to JSON |

| [`json_to_csv`](#json_to_csv) | JSON to CSV |

| [`xml_query`](#xml_query) | XML XPath query |
| [`yaml_to_json`](#yaml_to_json) | YAML to JSON |

<a id="css_format"></a>
### `css_format` — CSS beautify / minify

Beautify CSS (one declaration per line, one selector per line, blank line between top-level rules, nested @media blocks) or minify it. Strings, url(...) and escapes are kept intact; calc() spacing is preserved. Optional comment removal (/*! … */ kept). Give "text" or a file "path". Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/html-css-format/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `text` | string | no | The CSS as inline text. |
| `path` | string | no | Or: a path to a CSS file (UTF-8; use this for big inputs). |
| `mode` | "beautify" \| "minify" | no (default `"beautify"`) | "beautify" (default) or "minify". |
| `indent` | "2" \| "4" \| "tab" \| integer | no (default `"2"`) | Indentation: 2, 4, "tab" (or any number of spaces 0-8). |
| `removeComments` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Drop comments (/*! … */ license comments are kept). |
| `output` | string | no | Optional: also write the result to this file path (or folder ending in "/"). The result text is then left out of the reply. Results over 1,000,000 characters are always written to a file. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `inputBytes` | number |  |
| `outputBytes` | number |  |
| `lines` | number |  |
| `result` | string | The converted / formatted text (left out when written to a file). |
| `path` | string | Absolute path of the written file, when "output" was given or the result was too big to inline. |
| `bytes` | number | Size of the written file in bytes. |

**Example**

```json
{
  "text": "a,b{color:red;margin:0 auto}"
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "result": "a,\nb {\n  color: red;\n  margin: 0 auto;\n}"
}
```

<a id="csv_to_json"></a>
### `csv_to_json` — CSV to JSON

Convert CSV/TSV to JSON with an RFC 4180 parser (quoted fields, "" escapes, line breaks inside quotes, CR/LF/CRLF, BOM). Detects the delimiter (comma, semicolon, tab, pipe), uses the first row as headers (blank → column_N, duplicates → name_2), and can type values (numbers, true/false, null; long digit strings like IDs stay strings). Output: array of objects or array of arrays, pretty-printed. Give "text" or a file "path". Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/csv-json/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `text` | string | no | The CSV as inline text. |
| `path` | string | no | Or: a path to a CSV/TSV file (UTF-8; use this for big inputs). |
| `delimiter` | "auto" \| "," \| ";" \| "tab" \| "\t" \| "\|" | no (default `"auto"`) | Delimiter: "auto" (detect), ",", ";", "tab" or "\|". |
| `header` | boolean | no (default `true`) | First row is a header (default true). |
| `types` | boolean | no (default `true`) | Turn numbers, true/false and empty/null into JSON types (default true). |
| `shape` | "objects" \| "arrays" | no (default `"objects"`) | "objects" (one object per row) or "arrays" (rows as arrays). |
| `output` | string | no | Optional: also write the result to this file path (or folder ending in "/"). The result text is then left out of the reply. Results over 1,000,000 characters are always written to a file. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `rows` | number |  |
| `columns` | string[] |  |
| `delimiter` | string |  |
| `ragged` | boolean | Some rows have a different number of fields. |
| `result` | string | The converted / formatted text (left out when written to a file). |
| `path` | string | Absolute path of the written file, when "output" was given or the result was too big to inline. |
| `bytes` | number | Size of the written file in bytes. |

**Example**

```json
{
  "text": "id,zip,ok\n1,007,true"
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "rows": 1,
  "columns": [
    "id",
    "zip",
    "ok"
  ],
  "delimiter": "comma",
  "ragged": false,
  "result": "[\n  {\n    \"id\": 1,\n    \"zip\": \"007\",\n    \"ok\": true\n  }\n]"
}
```

<a id="curl_to_code"></a>
### `curl_to_code` — cURL to code

Convert a curl command (bash/zsh quoting incl. $'…', line continuations, "Copy as cURL" from browsers) into code for JavaScript fetch, axios, Python requests, PHP cURL, Go net/http or Node.js http/https: every target the website supports. Understands -X, -H, -d/--data-raw/--data-binary/--data-urlencode, --json, -F forms and files, -u, -b, -A, -e, -G, -I, -k, -L, -m, --connect-timeout, -x, -T, --compressed, --oauth2-bearer. Also returns the parsed request (secrets masked unless revealSecrets) and notes on what could not be converted. Only parses text; never sends the request. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/curl-convert/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `command` | string | yes | The curl command, e.g. "curl -X POST https://api.example.com -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{"a":1}'". |
| `language` | "fetch" \| "axios" \| "python" \| "php" \| "go" \| "node" \| "all" | no (default `"fetch"`) | Target: fetch (browser/Node fetch), axios, python (requests), php (cURL), go (net/http), node (http/https), or "all". |
| `revealSecrets` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Show Authorization/Cookie/API-key header values and the basic-auth password in the parsed summary (the code always contains them). |
| `output` | string | no | Optional: write the code to this file (single language only). |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `method` | string |  |
| `url` | string |  |
| `headers` | object[] |  |
| `body` | string \| null |  |
| `basicAuth` | object |  |
| `warnings` | string[] |  |
| `notConverted` | string[] | curl options with no equivalent in the generated code. |
| `code` | string |  |
| `codes` | object | With language "all": code per target. |
| `path` | string |  |
| `bytes` | number |  |

**Example** — requests.get(url, auth=("user", "pass"))

```json
{
  "command": "curl -u user:pass https://s3eidat.github.io",
  "language": "python"
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "method": "GET",
  "url": "https://s3eidat.github.io",
  "headers": [],
  "body": null,
  "basicAuth": {
    "user": "user",
    "password": "••••••••"
  },
  "warnings": [],
  "notConverted": [],
  "code": "import requests\n\nurl = \"https://s3eidat.github.io\"\n\nresponse = requests.get(url, auth=(\"user\", \"pass\"))\nprint(response.status_code)\nprint(response.text)"
}
```

<a id="html_format"></a>
### `html_format` — HTML beautify / minify

Beautify (indent block elements, keep inline runs on one line) or minify HTML with a forgiving tokenizer: implied end tags (<p>, <li>, <td>…), void elements, and <script>, <style>, <pre>, <textarea> contents kept byte-for-byte. Whitespace between attributes is normalised; attribute values are never changed. Optional comment removal (IE conditional comments kept). Text only: nothing is rendered or run. Give "text" or a file "path". Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/html-css-format/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `text` | string | no | The HTML as inline text. |
| `path` | string | no | Or: a path to a HTML file (UTF-8; use this for big inputs). |
| `mode` | "beautify" \| "minify" | no (default `"beautify"`) | "beautify" (default) or "minify". |
| `indent` | "2" \| "4" \| "tab" \| integer | no (default `"2"`) | Indentation: 2, 4, "tab" (or any number of spaces 0-8). |
| `removeComments` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Drop comments (IE conditional comments are kept). |
| `output` | string | no | Optional: also write the result to this file path (or folder ending in "/"). The result text is then left out of the reply. Results over 1,000,000 characters are always written to a file. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `inputBytes` | number |  |
| `outputBytes` | number |  |
| `lines` | number |  |
| `result` | string | The converted / formatted text (left out when written to a file). |
| `path` | string | Absolute path of the written file, when "output" was given or the result was too big to inline. |
| `bytes` | number | Size of the written file in bytes. |

**Example**

```json
{
  "text": "<div><p>Hi <b>there</b></p><ul><li>a<li>b</ul></div>"
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "result": "<div>\n  <p>Hi <b>there</b></p>\n  <ul>\n    <li>a\n    <li>b\n  </ul>\n</div>"
}
```

<a id="json_format"></a>
### `json_format` — JSON format / minify / validate

Validate, pretty-print (2 or 4 spaces, tab) or minify JSON, optionally sorting object keys (deeply). Invalid JSON gives a clear error with line and column and the lines around it. Also returns size, key count, depth and value count. Give "text" or a file "path"; "output" writes the result to a file. Uses the standard JSON parser (no comments or trailing commas). Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/json-studio/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `text` | string | no | The JSON as inline text. |
| `path` | string | no | Or: a path to a JSON file (UTF-8; use this for big inputs). |
| `mode` | "format" \| "minify" \| "validate" | no (default `"format"`) | "format" pretty-prints, "minify" removes all whitespace, "validate" only checks (never throws on bad JSON). |
| `indent` | "2" \| "4" \| "tab" \| integer | no (default `"2"`) | Indentation: 2, 4, "tab" (or any number of spaces 0-8). |
| `sortKeys` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Sort object keys alphabetically at every level. |
| `output` | string | no | Optional: also write the result to this file path (or folder ending in "/"). The result text is then left out of the reply. Results over 1,000,000 characters are always written to a file. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `valid` | boolean |  |
| `error` | string |  |
| `line` | number |  |
| `column` | number |  |
| `stats` | object |  |
| `result` | string | The converted / formatted text (left out when written to a file). |
| `path` | string | Absolute path of the written file, when "output" was given or the result was too big to inline. |
| `bytes` | number | Size of the written file in bytes. |

**Example**

```json
{
  "text": "{\"b\":1,\"a\":[1,2]}",
  "sortKeys": true
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "valid": true,
  "result": "{\n  \"a\": [\n    1,\n    2\n  ],\n  \"b\": 1\n}"
}
```

**Example** — valid: false, line 2, column 1

```json
{
  "text": "{\"a\":1,\n}",
  "mode": "validate"
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "valid": false,
  "error": "Expected double-quoted property name in JSON at position 8 (line 2 column 1)",
  "line": 2,
  "column": 1
}
```

<a id="json_query"></a>
### `json_query` — JSON query (JSONPath)

Pick values out of JSON with a JSONPath expression. Supported: $ (root, optional), .key, ['key'], [0], [-1], [*], .*, ..key (recursive), [start:end:step] slices, unions [0,2] / ['a','b'], and filters like [?(@.price < 10 && @.tags)] (==, !=, <, <=, >, >=, =~ /regex/i, &&, ||, !, .length). Returns each match with its path (the same path style the website's tree view copies, e.g. $.store.book[0].title). Give "text" or a file "path". Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/json-studio/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `text` | string | no | The JSON as inline text. |
| `path` | string | no | Or: a path to a JSON file (UTF-8; use this for big inputs). |
| `query` | string | yes | JSONPath expression, e.g. "$.store.book[*].author" or "$..book[?(@.price < 10)].title". |
| `limit` | integer | no (default `1000`) | Return at most this many matches (default 1000). |
| `output` | string | no | Optional: write the matched values as a JSON array to this file. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `count` | number | Total number of matches. |
| `matches` | object[] |  |
| `truncated` | boolean |  |
| `path` | string |  |
| `bytes` | number |  |

**Example**

```json
{
  "text": "{\"store\":{\"book\":[{\"title\":\"A\",\"price\":8},{\"title\":\"B\",\"price\":12}]}}",
  "query": "$..book[?(@.price < 10)].title"
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "count": 1,
  "matches": [
    {
      "path": "$.store.book[0].title",
      "value": "A"
    }
  ]
}
```

<a id="json_to_csv"></a>
### `json_to_csv` — JSON to CSV

Convert JSON to CSV. An array of objects becomes one row per object with nested objects/arrays flattened to dotted columns (address.city, tags.0); an array of arrays is written as-is. Fields are quoted only when needed (delimiter, quotes, line breaks, leading/trailing spaces); CRLF line ends. A formula guard (on by default) prefixes cells starting with = + - @ so spreadsheets don't run them. Give "text" or a file "path". Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/csv-json/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `text` | string | no | The JSON (array or object) as inline text. |
| `path` | string | no | Or: a path to a JSON file (UTF-8; use this for big inputs). |
| `delimiter` | "auto" \| "," \| ";" \| "tab" \| "\t" \| "\|" | no (default `"auto"`) | Delimiter: "auto" (comma), ",", ";", "tab" or "\|". |
| `header` | boolean | no (default `true`) | Include a header row (default true). |
| `safe` | boolean | no (default `true`) | Formula guard: prefix cells like =SUM(A1) with ' (default true). |
| `bom` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Start with a UTF-8 BOM so Excel reads non-English text correctly. |
| `output` | string | no | Optional: also write the result to this file path (or folder ending in "/"). The result text is then left out of the reply. Results over 1,000,000 characters are always written to a file. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `rows` | number |  |
| `columns` | string[] \| null |  |
| `result` | string | The converted / formatted text (left out when written to a file). |
| `path` | string | Absolute path of the written file, when "output" was given or the result was too big to inline. |
| `bytes` | number | Size of the written file in bytes. |

**Example**

```json
{
  "text": "[{\"a\":{\"b\":1},\"s\":\"say \\\"hi\\\"\"}]"
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "rows": 1,
  "columns": [
    "a.b",
    "s"
  ],
  "result": "a.b,s\r\n1,\"say \"\"hi\"\"\"\r\n"
}
```

<a id="json_to_types"></a>
### `json_to_types` — JSON to types (TypeScript, Go, Python, Zod)

Generate type definitions from a JSON sample: TypeScript interfaces (or type aliases), Go structs with json tags (initialisms like ID/URL, pointers for optional/nullable, optional omitempty), Python dataclasses (snake_case fields with the original key noted) and Zod schemas. All items of an array are merged into one type (missing keys become optional, null makes a field nullable, int+float → number/float64, mixed values → unions); identical shapes share one name. These four are every language the website supports. Give "text" or a file "path". Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/json-to-types/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `text` | string | no | The JSON sample as inline text. |
| `path` | string | no | Or: a path to a JSON file (UTF-8; use this for big inputs). |
| `language` | "typescript" \| "go" \| "python" \| "zod" \| "all" | no (default `"typescript"`) | Output language, or "all" for all four. |
| `rootName` | string | no (default `"Root"`) | Name of the top-level type (turned into PascalCase). |
| `export` | boolean | no (default `true`) | TypeScript / Zod: add the export keyword. |
| `tsTypeAlias` | boolean | no (default `false`) | TypeScript: use "type X = {…}" instead of "interface X {…}". |
| `goOmitEmpty` | boolean | no (default `true`) | Go: add ,omitempty to optional fields. |
| `output` | string | no | Optional: write the code to this file (with language "all", the four outputs joined by headers). |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `types` | number | Number of named object types. |
| `typescript` | string |  |
| `go` | string |  |
| `python` | string |  |
| `zod` | string |  |
| `result` | string | The converted / formatted text (left out when written to a file). |
| `path` | string | Absolute path of the written file, when "output" was given or the result was too big to inline. |
| `bytes` | number | Size of the written file in bytes. |

**Example**

```json
{
  "text": "[{\"a\":1},{\"b\":true}]",
  "rootName": "Root",
  "export": false
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "types": 1,
  "result": "type Root = RootItem[];\n\ninterface RootItem {\n  a?: number;\n  b?: boolean;\n}\n"
}
```

<a id="json_to_yaml"></a>
### `json_to_yaml` — JSON to YAML

Convert JSON to YAML (js-yaml dump: no line wrapping, no anchors; strings that would read as numbers/booleans are quoted). Options: 2 or 4 space indent, sort keys, and write a top-level array as a multi-document YAML stream (--- per item). Invalid JSON gives line and column. Give "text" or a file "path". Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/yaml-json/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `text` | string | no | The JSON as inline text. |
| `path` | string | no | Or: a path to a JSON file (UTF-8; use this for big inputs). |
| `indent` | 2 \| 4 \| "2" \| "4" | no (default `2`) | Indentation: 2 or 4 spaces. |
| `sortKeys` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Sort mapping keys alphabetically. |
| `multiDocument` | boolean | no (default `false`) | A top-level JSON array becomes one YAML document per item. |
| `output` | string | no | Optional: also write the result to this file path (or folder ending in "/"). The result text is then left out of the reply. Results over 1,000,000 characters are always written to a file. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `documents` | number |  |
| `result` | string | The converted / formatted text (left out when written to a file). |
| `path` | string | Absolute path of the written file, when "output" was given or the result was too big to inline. |
| `bytes` | number | Size of the written file in bytes. |

**Example**

```json
{
  "text": "{\"b\":1,\"a\":{\"d\":1,\"c\":2}}",
  "sortKeys": true
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "documents": 1,
  "result": "a:\n  c: 2\n  d: 1\nb: 1\n"
}
```

<a id="markdown_table"></a>
### `markdown_table` — Markdown table

Make a GitHub-flavoured Markdown table from CSV, TSV, a JSON array (of objects or of arrays), an existing Markdown table (to re-align / re-pad it) or a "rows" array. Per-column alignment (left, center, right), columns padded to line up (optional), pipes escaped as \| and line breaks as <br>. CSV delimiter (comma, semicolon, tab) is detected from the first line. Give "text", a file "path" or "rows". Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/markdown-table/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `text` | string | no | The CSV / TSV / JSON / Markdown table as inline text. |
| `path` | string | no | Or: a path to a CSV/TSV/JSON/Markdown file (UTF-8; use this for big inputs). |
| `rows` | ((string \| number \| boolean \| null)[])[] | no | Or: the table as an array of rows (first row = header unless header is false). |
| `format` | "auto" \| "csv" \| "tsv" \| "json" \| "markdown" | no (default `"auto"`) | Input format; "auto" detects Markdown, then JSON, then CSV/TSV. |
| `header` | boolean | no (default `true`) | First row is the header. When false, "Column 1", "Column 2"… headers are added. |
| `align` | "none" \| "left" \| "center" \| "right" \| ("none" \| "left" \| "center" \| "right")[] | no | Column alignment: one value for all columns or one per column. Default: keep a Markdown input's alignment, else none. |
| `pretty` | boolean | no (default `true`) | Pad cells so the columns line up (default true). |
| `output` | string | no | Optional: also write the result to this file path (or folder ending in "/"). The result text is then left out of the reply. Results over 1,000,000 characters are always written to a file. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `columns` | number |  |
| `rows` | number | Body rows (without the header). |
| `result` | string | The converted / formatted text (left out when written to a file). |
| `path` | string | Absolute path of the written file, when "output" was given or the result was too big to inline. |
| `bytes` | number | Size of the written file in bytes. |

**Example**

```json
{
  "text": "Name,Qty\nApple,3",
  "align": [
    "left",
    "right"
  ]
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "columns": 2,
  "rows": 1,
  "result": "| Name  | Qty |\n| :---- | --: |\n| Apple |   3 |"
}
```

<a id="markdown_table_parse"></a>
### `markdown_table_parse` — Markdown table to JSON / CSV

Read a Markdown table (pipes optional at the ends, \| escapes, <br> as line breaks) back into data: JSON objects keyed by the header, JSON arrays, or CSV; also returns each column's alignment. Uses the website's Markdown import. Give "text" or a file "path". Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/markdown-table/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `text` | string | no | The Markdown table as inline text. |
| `path` | string | no | Or: a path to a Markdown file (UTF-8; use this for big inputs). |
| `as` | "objects" \| "arrays" \| "csv" | no (default `"objects"`) | "objects" (header → keys), "arrays" (header row first) or "csv". |
| `output` | string | no | Optional: also write the result to this file path (or folder ending in "/"). The result text is then left out of the reply. Results over 1,000,000 characters are always written to a file. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `columns` | string[] |  |
| `aligns` | ("none" \| "left" \| "center" \| "right")[] |  |
| `rows` | number |  |
| `data` | object[] | The rows (JSON formats, when not written to a file). |
| `result` | string | The converted / formatted text (left out when written to a file). |
| `path` | string | Absolute path of the written file, when "output" was given or the result was too big to inline. |
| `bytes` | number | Size of the written file in bytes. |

**Example**

```json
{
  "text": "| Name | Qty |\n| :-- | --: |\n| Apple | 3 |"
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "columns": [
    "Name",
    "Qty"
  ],
  "aligns": [
    "left",
    "right"
  ],
  "rows": 1,
  "data": [
    {
      "Name": "Apple",
      "Qty": "3"
    }
  ]
}
```

<a id="sql_format"></a>
### `sql_format` — SQL format / minify

Format SQL (one clause per line, indented sub-queries, CASE blocks, JOIN … ON with AND/OR, CREATE TABLE column lists) or minify it to one line per statement. Options: keyword case (UPPER, lower, keep), indent (2, 4, tab), trailing or leading commas, keep/drop comments when minifying. Dialect-neutral tokenizer: understands -- and /* */ comments, '' and E'\'' strings, "x", `x` and [x] identifiers, $$ dollar quotes, ::casts, JSON operators, :name/$1/? parameters. Only re-spaces; never runs anything. Give "text" or a file "path". Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/sql-format/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `text` | string | no | The SQL as inline text. |
| `path` | string | no | Or: a path to a .sql file (UTF-8; use this for big inputs). |
| `mode` | "format" \| "minify" | no (default `"format"`) | "format" (default) or "minify". |
| `keywordCase` | "upper" \| "lower" \| "preserve" | no (default `"upper"`) | Keyword and built-in function case. |
| `indent` | "2" \| "4" \| "tab" \| 2 \| 4 | no (default `"2"`) | Indentation: 2, 4 or "tab". |
| `commas` | "trailing" \| "leading" | no (default `"trailing"`) | Put list commas at line ends (trailing) or starts (leading). |
| `keepComments` | boolean | no (default `true`) | Minify only: keep comments (as /* */). Optimizer hints /*+ */ and /*! */ are always kept. |
| `output` | string | no | Optional: also write the result to this file path (or folder ending in "/"). The result text is then left out of the reply. Results over 1,000,000 characters are always written to a file. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `statements` | number |  |
| `lines` | number |  |
| `warning` | string | Set when a quote or comment is never closed. |
| `result` | string | The converted / formatted text (left out when written to a file). |
| `path` | string | Absolute path of the written file, when "output" was given or the result was too big to inline. |
| `bytes` | number | Size of the written file in bytes. |

**Example**

```json
{
  "text": "select a, b from t where x = 1 and y = 2"
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "statements": 1,
  "lines": 8,
  "result": "SELECT\n  a,\n  b\nFROM\n  t\nWHERE\n  x = 1\n  AND y = 2"
}
```

<a id="xml_format"></a>
### `xml_format` — XML format / minify / validate / to JSON

Validate XML (well-formedness and namespaces, with line and column for errors), pretty-print it (2, 4 spaces or tab; mixed content, CDATA and xml:space="preserve" kept exactly as written), minify it, or convert it to JSON (attributes as "@name", repeated elements as arrays, text as "#text"). Optionally removes comments. Never loads external DTDs or entities. Give "text" or a file "path". Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/xml-format/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `text` | string | no | The XML as inline text. |
| `path` | string | no | Or: a path to a XML file (UTF-8; use this for big inputs). |
| `mode` | "format" \| "minify" \| "json" \| "validate" | no (default `"format"`) | "format" pretty-prints, "minify" strips layout whitespace, "json" converts to JSON, "validate" only checks (never throws on bad XML). |
| `indent` | "2" \| "4" \| "tab" \| integer | no (default `"2"`) | Indentation: 2, 4, "tab" (or any number of spaces 0-8). |
| `removeComments` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Drop <!-- comments --> (format and minify). |
| `output` | string | no | Optional: also write the result to this file path (or folder ending in "/"). The result text is then left out of the reply. Results over 1,000,000 characters are always written to a file. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `valid` | boolean |  |
| `error` | string |  |
| `line` | number |  |
| `column` | number |  |
| `elements` | number |  |
| `depth` | number |  |
| `result` | string | The converted / formatted text (left out when written to a file). |
| `path` | string | Absolute path of the written file, when "output" was given or the result was too big to inline. |
| `bytes` | number | Size of the written file in bytes. |

**Example**

```json
{
  "text": "<r><a>1</a><b x='1'  y=\"2\"><c/></b></r>"
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "valid": true,
  "elements": 4,
  "depth": 3,
  "result": "<r>\n  <a>1</a>\n  <b x='1' y=\"2\">\n    <c/>\n  </b>\n</r>"
}
```

**Example**

```json
{
  "text": "<a>\n  <b></a>",
  "mode": "validate"
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "valid": false,
  "error": "Opening and ending tag mismatch: b line 2 and a",
  "line": 2,
  "column": 6
}
```

<a id="xml_query"></a>
### `xml_query` — XML XPath query

Run an XPath 1.0 expression on XML: node-sets (elements, attributes, text…), numbers (count(), sum()), strings and booleans. Full XPath 1.0: all axes, predicates, operators and the core function library. Namespace prefixes declared anywhere in the document can be used as-is; a default namespace on the root gets the prefix "d" (e.g. //d:book), as on the website. Each matched node comes back as XML text plus its type, name and XPath location. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/xml-format/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `text` | string | no | The XML as inline text. |
| `path` | string | no | Or: a path to a XML file (UTF-8; use this for big inputs). |
| `xpath` | string | yes | XPath 1.0 expression, e.g. "//book[price > 30]/title", "count(//item)", "//d:entry/@id". |
| `namespaces` | object | no | Extra or overriding prefix → namespace URI bindings, e.g. {"atom":"http://www.w3.org/2005/Atom"}. |
| `limit` | integer | no (default `1000`) | Return at most this many nodes (default 1000). |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `kind` | "nodes" \| "number" \| "string" \| "boolean" |  |
| `value` | number \| string \| boolean | For number / string / boolean results. |
| `count` | number | Number of matched nodes. |
| `nodes` | object[] |  |
| `truncated` | boolean |  |
| `namespaces` | object | Prefixes available to the expression. |
| `hint` | string |  |

**Example**

```json
{
  "text": "<lib><book><price>42</price><title>A</title></book><book><price>9</price><title>B</title></book></lib>",
  "xpath": "//book[price > 30]/title"
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "kind": "nodes",
  "count": 1,
  "nodes": [
    {
      "type": "element",
      "name": "title",
      "location": "/lib/book[1]/title",
      "text": "<title>A</title>"
    }
  ]
}
```

<a id="yaml_to_json"></a>
### `yaml_to_json` — YAML to JSON

Convert YAML to JSON (YAML 1.2 core schema plus anchors/aliases, << merge keys, timestamps, multi-document files). Safe: no code tags (!!js/function, !!python… are rejected), alias bombs and recursive aliases are stopped. Several documents become a JSON array. Errors give line and column. Give "text" or a file "path". Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/yaml-json/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `text` | string | no | The YAML as inline text. |
| `path` | string | no | Or: a path to a YAML file (UTF-8; use this for big inputs). |
| `indent` | 2 \| 4 \| "2" \| "4" | no (default `2`) | Indentation: 2 or 4 spaces. |
| `sortKeys` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Sort object keys alphabetically at every level. |
| `alwaysArray` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Always output an array of documents, even for one document. |
| `output` | string | no | Optional: also write the result to this file path (or folder ending in "/"). The result text is then left out of the reply. Results over 1,000,000 characters are always written to a file. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `documents` | number |  |
| `values` | number |  |
| `result` | string | The converted / formatted text (left out when written to a file). |
| `path` | string | Absolute path of the written file, when "output" was given or the result was too big to inline. |
| `bytes` | number | Size of the written file in bytes. |

**Example**

```json
{
  "text": "base: &b {x: 1}\nchild:\n  <<: *b\n  y: 2\n"
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "documents": 1,
  "values": 6,
  "result": "{\n  \"base\": {\n    \"x\": 1\n  },\n  \"child\": {\n    \"x\": 1,\n    \"y\": 2\n  }\n}\n"
}
```

<a id="base_convert"></a>
### `base_convert` — Number base convert

Convert a whole number of any size (BigInt) between bases 2–36: binary, octal, decimal, hex, base 36 or any other. Input base can be auto-detected from 0x / 0b / 0o prefixes; spaces, _ and , separators are ignored; negatives OK. Also shows bit length and the two's complement view (signed/unsigned value, hex, bits) at 8/16/32/64 bits, and whether it fits. Fractions are not supported (same as the website). Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/base-convert/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `value` | string | yes | The number, e.g. "0xC0FFEE", "-0b101", "1_000_000", "zz" (with from=36). |
| `from` | "auto" \| integer | no (default `"auto"`) | Input base 2–36, or "auto" (prefix, else decimal). |
| `to` | integer[] | no (default `[2,8,10,16,36]`) | Output bases. Default [2, 8, 10, 16, 36]. |
| `group` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Group digits like the website (binary/hex by 4, octal by 3, decimal with commas). |
| `uppercase` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Upper-case letters for bases above 10. |
| `width` | 8 \| 16 \| 32 \| 64 | no (default `32`) | Bit width for the two's complement view. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `decimal` | string |  |
| `results` | object[] |  |
| `bitLength` | number |  |
| `bytes` | number |  |
| `twosComplement` | object |  |

**Example** — decimal 12648430, binary 110000001111111111101110

```json
{
  "value": "0xC0FFEE"
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "decimal": "12648430",
  "results": [
    {
      "base": 2,
      "name": "Binary",
      "value": "110000001111111111101110"
    },
    {
      "base": 8,
      "name": "Octal",
      "value": "60177756"
    },
    {
      "base": 10,
      "name": "Decimal",
      "value": "12648430"
    },
    {
      "base": 16,
      "name": "Hex",
      "value": "c0ffee"
    },
    {
      "base": 36,
      "name": "Base 36",
      "value": "7j3la"
    }
  ],
  "bitLength": 24,
  "bytes": 3,
  "twosComplement": {
    "width": 32,
    "signed": "12648430",
    "unsigned": "12648430",
    "hex": "0x00C0 FFEE",
    "binary": "00000000110000001111111111101110",
    "fits": true,
    "note": "Fits in 32 bits. Signed range -2147483648 to 2147483647."
  }
}
```

**Example** — two’s complement hex FF

```json
{
  "value": "-1",
  "width": 8
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "decimal": "-1",
  "results": [
    {
      "base": 2,
      "name": "Binary",
      "value": "-1"
    },
    {
      "base": 8,
      "name": "Octal",
      "value": "-1"
    },
    {
      "base": 10,
      "name": "Decimal",
      "value": "-1"
    },
    {
      "base": 16,
      "name": "Hex",
      "value": "-1"
    },
    {
      "base": 36,
      "name": "Base 36",
      "value": "-1"
    }
  ],
  "bitLength": 1,
  "bytes": 1,
  "twosComplement": {
    "width": 8,
    "signed": "-1",
    "unsigned": "255",
    "hex": "0xFF",
    "binary": "11111111",
    "fits": true,
    "note": "Fits in 8 bits. Signed range -128 to 127."
  }
}
```

<a id="base64_decode"></a>
### `base64_decode` — Base64 decode

Decode Base64 (standard or URL-safe, padding optional, whitespace and data: URI prefix allowed). Valid UTF-8 text is returned inline; binary data (or anything when "output" is given) is written to a file whose extension comes from the detected type (PNG, JPEG, GIF, WebP, BMP, PDF, ZIP, gzip, MP3, Ogg, MP4, WOFF2, SVG, JSON). Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/base64-kit/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `base64` | string | yes | The Base64 text to decode (may be a data: URI). |
| `output` | string | no | Where to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set). |
| `fileName` | string | no | Base name for the written file (extension follows the detected type). Default "decoded". |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `text` | string | Decoded UTF-8 text (when the bytes are text and no output path was given). |
| `path` | string | Written file, for binary data or when "output" was given. |
| `bytes` | number |  |
| `isText` | boolean |  |
| `detectedType` | string |  |
| `mimeType` | string |  |
| `dataUriMime` | string | MIME type the data: URI declared, if any. |

**Example**

```json
{
  "base64": "Zm9vYmFy"
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "text": "foobar",
  "bytes": 6,
  "isText": true,
  "detectedType": "Text",
  "mimeType": "text/plain"
}
```

**Example** — Writes decoded.png

```json
{
  "base64": "data:image/png;base64,iVBORw0KGgoAAAANSUhEUgAAAAEAAAABCAYAAAAfFcSJAAAADUlEQVR42mNkYPhfDwAChwGA60e6kgAAAABJRU5ErkJggg=="
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "bytes": 70,
  "isText": false,
  "detectedType": "PNG image",
  "mimeType": "image/png",
  "dataUriMime": "image/png",
  "path": "/tmp/s3eidat-tools/decoded.png"
}
```

<a id="base64_encode"></a>
### `base64_encode` — Base64 encode

Encode text (as UTF-8) or a file (path or base64) to Base64. Options: URL-safe alphabet (- and _, no padding), wrap at 76 columns (MIME), or a data: URI for files (MIME type detected from magic bytes). Results over 1 MB, or when "output" is given, are written to a text file instead of returned inline. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/base64-kit/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `text` | string | no | Text to encode (UTF-8). |
| `file` | string | no | Or: a file to encode: local path or base64 data. |
| `urlSafe` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Use the URL-safe alphabet (- and _) and drop "=" padding. |
| `wrap` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Insert a line break every 76 characters (MIME style). |
| `dataUri` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Return a data: URI (data:<mime>;base64,…) instead of bare Base64. |
| `output` | string | no | Where to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set). |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `base64` | string | The encoded text (absent when written to a file). |
| `path` | string | File holding the encoded text, when written to disk. |
| `inputBytes` | number |  |
| `characters` | number |  |
| `detectedType` | string |  |
| `mimeType` | string |  |

**Example**

```json
{
  "text": "foobar"
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "base64": "Zm9vYmFy",
  "inputBytes": 6,
  "characters": 8
}
```

**Example** — URL-safe output without padding

```json
{
  "text": "ûÿ",
  "urlSafe": true
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "inputBytes": 4,
  "characters": 6,
  "base64": "w7vDvw"
}
```

<a id="bytes_convert"></a>
### `bytes_convert` — Text ⇄ UTF-8 bytes

Turn text into its UTF-8 bytes (hex, binary or decimal), or bytes back into text. Byte input is lenient: "48 65", "4865", "0x48,0x69", "01001000 01101001", "72 105". Invalid UTF-8 is reported and shown with � replacements. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/base-convert/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `text` | string | no | Text to turn into bytes. |
| `bytes` | string | no | Or: bytes to turn into text, in the chosen format. |
| `format` | "hex" \| "bin" \| "dec" | no (default `"hex"`) | Byte format. Default hex. |
| `uppercase` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Upper-case hex digits. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `text` | string |  |
| `bytes` | string |  |
| `byteCount` | number |  |
| `characters` | number |  |
| `validUtf8` | boolean |  |

**Example**

```json
{
  "text": "Hi ☕",
  "uppercase": true
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "text": "Hi ☕",
  "bytes": "48 69 20 E2 98 95",
  "byteCount": 6,
  "characters": 4,
  "validUtf8": true
}
```

<a id="chmod_calc"></a>
### `chmod_calc` — chmod calculator

Convert Unix file permissions between octal (755, 0644, 4755), symbolic (rwxr-xr-x, ls -l style -rwsr-xr-x) and chmod clauses (u=rwx,g=rx,o=; relative ones like g+w or +x apply to "base"). Returns all forms, a plain-English description per owner/group/others, setuid/setgid/sticky meaning, safety warnings, and ready chmod commands. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/chmod-calc/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `mode` | string | yes | Octal ("755", "0644", "2775"), symbolic ("rwxr-x---", "drwxrwxrwt") or clauses ("u=rw,go=r", "+x"). |
| `base` | string | no | Starting mode (octal) for relative clauses like "g+w". Default 000. |
| `file` | string | no (default `"file"`) | File name for the example chmod commands (shell-quoted). |
| `recursive` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Add -R to the example commands. |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `octal` | string |  |
| `symbolic` | string |  |
| `clauses` | string |  |
| `description` | object[] |  |
| `warnings` | object[] |  |
| `commands` | object |  |

**Example** — symbolic rwsr-xr-x, clauses u=rwxs,g=rx,o=rx, setuid explained

```json
{
  "mode": "4755"
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "octal": "4755",
  "symbolic": "rwsr-xr-x",
  "clauses": "u=rwxs,g=rx,o=rx",
  "description": [
    {
      "who": "Owner",
      "text": "can read, write and execute"
    },
    {
      "who": "Group",
      "text": "can read and execute"
    },
    {
      "who": "Others",
      "text": "can read and execute"
    },
    {
      "who": "setuid",
      "text": "Runs as the file's owner"
    }
  ],
  "warnings": [],
  "commands": {
    "octal": "chmod 4755 file",
    "symbolic": "chmod u=rwxs,g=rx,o=rx file"
  }
}
```

**Example** — octal 750

```json
{
  "mode": "g-w,o-rwx",
  "base": "775",
  "file": "/srv/app"
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "octal": "750",
  "symbolic": "rwxr-x---",
  "clauses": "u=rwx,g=rx,o=",
  "description": [
    {
      "who": "Owner",
      "text": "can read, write and execute"
    },
    {
      "who": "Group",
      "text": "can read and execute"
    },
    {
      "who": "Others",
      "text": "has no access"
    }
  ],
  "warnings": [],
  "commands": {
    "octal": "chmod 750 /srv/app",
    "symbolic": "chmod u=rwx,g=rx,o= /srv/app"
  }
}
```

<a id="cron_explain"></a>
### `cron_explain` — Cron explain

Explain a standard 5-field cron expression (minute hour day-of-month month day-of-week; names like MON/JAN, ranges, lists, steps, ? and the @hourly/@daily/@weekly/@monthly/@yearly/@reboot macros) in plain English and list the next run times in an IANA timezone. Follows Vixie cron: when both day fields are restricted a day matches either one. Quartz 6/7-field and L/W/# syntax are rejected with a clear message; per-field errors are reported. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/cron-explain/

Hints: readOnly, idempotent

**Inputs**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `expression` | string | yes | The cron expression, e.g. "*/30 9-17 * * 1-5" or "@daily". |
| `count` | integer | no (default `10`) | How many upcoming run times to list (0–100). Default 10. |
| `timezone` | string | no | IANA timezone the cron daemon runs in. Default: the server's (UTC). |
| `from` | string | no | Start searching after this instant (ISO 8601). Default: now. |
| `hour12` | boolean | no (default `false`) | Describe times on a 12-hour clock (9:00 AM) instead of 24-hour (09:00). |

**Output**

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `valid` | boolean |  |
| `description` | string |  |
| `expanded` | string | The 5 fields after expanding a macro. |
| `macro` | string |  |
| `errors` | object | Per-field error messages (minute, hour, dom, month, dow). |
| `error` | string |  |
| `timezone` | string |  |
| `nextRuns` | object[] |  |
| `note` | string |  |

**Example** — description: "At 09:00, on Monday through Friday"

```json
{
  "expression": "0 9 * * 1-5",
  "count": 3,
  "timezone": "Europe/London"
}
```

Result:

```json
{
  "valid": true,
  "description": "At 09:00, on Monday through Friday.",
  "timezone": "Europe/London",
  "expanded": "0 9 * * 1-5",
  "nextRuns": [
    {
      "iso": "2026-01-16T09:00:00+00:00",
      "utc": "2026-01-16T09:00:00.000Z",
      "local": "Fri, Jan 16, 2026, 09:00",
      "relative": "in 24 hours"
    },
    {
      "iso": "2026-01-19T09:00:00+00:00",
      "utc": "2026-01-19T09:00:00.000Z",
      "local": "Mon, Jan 19, 2026, 09:00",
      "relative": "in 4 days"
    },
    {
      "iso": "2026-01-20T09:00:00+00:00",
      "utc": "2026-01-20T09:00:00.000Z",
      "local": "Tue, Jan 20, 2026, 09:00",
      "relative": "in 5 days"
    }
  ]
}
```

**Example** — description: "At 00:00, on day 13 of the month or on Friday"

```json
{
  "expression": "0 0 13 * 5"
}
```

```json
{
```
